{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01RFTGmvx6hbB5ngn7etmRzo","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→```markdown\n     2→# LedgerQuest Engine — Architecture Overview\n     3→_A serverless, business-grade game framework for interactive SaaS experiences_\n     4→\n     5→---\n     6→\n     7→## 1. Architectural Tenets\n     8→1. **Serverless–First**  \n     9→   Every execution path is decomposed into fine-grained, stateless Lambdas, Step Functions, or Fargate tasks. This removes all **always-on** costs and yields “pay-for-play” economics.\n    10→\n    11→2. **Event-Driven Everything**  \n    12→   Scene mutations, player inputs, AI ticks, and business telemetry ride the same EventBridge bus enabling loose-coupling, auditability, and near-infinite horizontal scale.\n    13→\n    14→3. **Enterprise-Grade Governance**  \n    15→   Multi-tenant isolation, field-level encryption, and immutable audit logs are baked in through KMS, DynamoDB Streams, and CloudTrail integration—**without** sacrificing < 20 ms game-loop latency.\n    16→\n    17→4. **Pure ECS (Entity-Component-System)**  \n    18→   Engine internals embrace a data-oriented ECS that maps cleanly onto distributed storage (DynamoDB) and compute (Lambda). Functional “Systems” are individually deployable as versioned Lambda layers.\n    19→\n    20→---\n    21→\n    22→## 2. High-Level System Diagram\n    23→\n    24→```mermaid\n    25→flowchart TD\n    26→    subgraph Client Tier\n    27→        WebGL -->|WebSocket| APIGW_WS\n    28→        Unity3D -->|REST/GraphQL| APIGW_HTTP\n    29→    end\n    30→\n    31→    subgraph Control Plane\n    32→        CI/CD --> CodePipeline --> {Canary Tests}\n    33→    end\n    34→\n    35→    subgraph Game Plane\n    36→        APIGW_WS --> Lambda_InputRouter\n    37→        APIGW_HTTP --> Lambda_InputRouter\n    38→        Lambda_InputRouter --> EventBridge\n    39→        EventBridge -->|ai-tick| Lambda_AI\n    40→        EventBridge -->|physics-tick| Lambda_Physics\n    41→        EventBridge -->|scene-save| Lambda_Serializer\n    42→        EventBridge --> StepFunction_GameLoop\n    43→        StepFunction_GameLoop --> Lambda_AI\n    44→        StepFunction_GameLoop --> Lambda_Physics\n    45→        StepFunction_GameLoop --> Fargate_GPU\n    46→    end\n    47→\n    48→    subgraph Data Plane\n    49→        DynamoDB[(ECS Tables)]\n    50→        S3[(Assets + Saves)]\n    51→        Timestream[(Telemetry)]\n    52→        DynamoDBStreams --> Lambda_Audit\n    53→    end\n    54→\n    55→    Lambda_AI --> DynamoDB\n    56→    Lambda_Physics --> DynamoDB\n    57→    Lambda_Serializer --> S3\n    58→    Fargate_GPU --> S3\n    59→```\n    60→\n    61→---\n    62→\n    63→## 3. Layered Architecture\n    64→\n    65→1. **Presentation Layer**  \n    66→   • Thin clients (React + Three.js, Unity, or native)  \n    67→   • State updates via WebSockets; snapshots via pre-signed S3 URLs  \n    68→\n    69→2. **Orchestration Layer**  \n    70→   • AWS Step Functions manage deterministic game loops (`≤ 60 Hz`)  \n    71→   • Each Lambda/System is idempotent & side-effect-free outside DynamoDB  \n    72→\n    73→3. **Domain Layer**  \n    74→   • ECS implementation (`Entity`, `Component`, `System`)  \n    75→   • Command bus & Observer pattern enable scripting & modding  \n    76→\n    77→4. **Infrastructure Layer**  \n    78→   • AWS CDK Type-Safe IaC (Python)  \n    79→   • Multi-account, multi-region, blue-green deployments  \n    80→\n    81→---\n    82→\n    83→## 4. Component Responsibility Matrix\n    84→\n    85→| Concern                | Service / Pattern                  | Rationale                                    |\n    86→|------------------------|------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|\n    87→| Physics Simulation     | `Lambda_Physics` (+ Layers: PyBullet) | Deterministic, easily parallelisable         |\n    88→| AI Behaviour Trees     | `Lambda_AI` (+ Layer: PyBehaviour) | Fine-grained per-Entity decision loops       |\n    89→| Scripting System       | `Lambda_Scripting` (Python / Lua)  | Hot-swappable via Layer versioning           |\n    90→| Rendering (Burst)      | `Fargate_GPU` (NV T4g)             | Short-lived cinematic renders & thumbnails   |\n    91→| Level Editor           | `AppSync + Lambda_Graph`           | Real-time collaboration with CRDTs           |\n    92→| Audit / Compliance     | `Lambda_Audit` + Timestream        | Immutable, query-ready logs                  |\n    93→\n    94→---\n    95→\n    96→## 5. Data Model (ECS Tables)\n    97→\n    98→```plaintext\n    99→PartitionKey          SortKey               JSON Payload\n   100→────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n   101→TENANT#<id>           ENTITY#<uuid>         { \"components\": { ... } }\n   102→TENANT#<id>           COMPONENT#Physics     { \"mass\": 1.2, ... }\n   103→TENANT#<id>           COMPONENT#Transform   { \"pos\": [x,y,z], ... }\n   104→```\n   105→\n   106→*Each component type is stored as a distinct item to maximise targeted updates and **avoid hot partitions**.*\n   107→\n   108→---\n   109→\n   110→## 6. Game Loop Orchestration (Step Functions)\n   111→\n   112→```mermaid\n   113→stateDiagram-v2\n   114→    [*] --> FrameStart\n   115→    FrameStart --> PhysicsStep: Emit \"physics-tick\"\n   116→    PhysicsStep --> AIStep: Emit \"ai-tick\"\n   117→    AIStep --> RenderStep: Publish \"gpu-render\" (optional)\n   118→    RenderStep --> SaveSnapshot: 1/60 probability\n   119→    SaveSnapshot --> FrameEnd\n   120→    FrameEnd --> [*]\n   121→```\n   122→\n   123→• **Max Duration**: 16 ms per tick (60 FPS target)  \n   124→• **Timeout Strategy**: Any System Lambda > 10 ms triggers circuit-breaker → degrade to 30 FPS for that tenant only.\n   125→\n   126→---\n   127→\n   128→## 7. Sample System Lambda (Physics)\n   129→\n   130→```python\n   131→# file: ledgerquest/systems/physics.py\n   132→import json\n   133→import os\n   134→from decimal import Decimal\n   135→from typing import Dict, Any\n   136→\n   137→import boto3\n   138→import pybullet as pb\n   139→from botocore.config import Config\n   140→\n   141→DYNAMO = boto3.resource(\n   142→    \"dynamodb\",\n   143→    config=Config(retries={\"max_attempts\": 3, \"mode\": \"standard\"})\n   144→).Table(os.environ[\"ECS_TABLE\"])\n   145→\n   146→def lambda_handler(event: Dict[str, Any], _context) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n   147→    \"\"\"\n   148→    Apply a single physics tick to the subset of entities that have\n   149→    both Transform and Physics components.\n   150→    `event` originates from EventBridge \"physics-tick\".\n   151→    \"\"\"\n   152→    tenant_id = event[\"detail\"][\"tenant_id\"]\n   153→    try:\n   154→        entities = _fetch_entities(tenant_id)\n   155→        updated = _simulate(entities)\n   156→        _persist(tenant_id, updated)\n   157→        return {\"status\": \"ok\", \"count\": len(updated)}\n   158→    except Exception as exc:            # noqa: BLE001\n   159→        # Forward to DLQ for inspection; preserve idempotency by *not* re-raising\n   160→        _send_to_dlq(event, exc)\n   161→        return {\"status\": \"error\", \"message\": str(exc)}\n   162→\n   163→def _fetch_entities(tenant_id: str) -> list[dict]:\n   164→    \"\"\"Query DynamoDB for all Physics components within the current shard.\"\"\"\n   165→    resp = DYNAMO.query(\n   166→        KeyConditionExpression=\"PartitionKey = :tenant AND begins_with(SortKey, :prefix)\",\n   167→        ExpressionAttributeValues={\":tenant\": f\"TENANT#{tenant_id}\", \":prefix\": \"COMPONENT#Physics\"},\n   168→        ConsistentRead=False,\n   169→    )\n   170→    return resp[\"Items\"]\n   171→\n   172→def _simulate(entities: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:\n   173→    \"\"\"Run deterministic physics and return updated transforms.\"\"\"\n   174→    pb.connect(pb.DIRECT)  # No GUI in Lambda\n   175→    updated: list[dict] = []\n   176→    for comp in entities:\n   177→        e_id = comp[\"EntityId\"]\n   178→        mass = float(comp[\"mass\"])\n   179→        # Simplified example; real implementation would batch bodies\n   180→        vel_x, vel_y, vel_z = 0.0, -9.81, 0.0\n   181→        pos = comp[\"pos\"]\n   182→        new_pos = [pos[0] + vel_x, pos[1] + vel_y * 0.016, pos[2] + vel_z]\n   183→        updated.append({\"EntityId\": e_id, \"pos\": new_pos})\n   184→    pb.disconnect()\n   185→    return updated\n   186→\n   187→def _persist(tenant_id: str, entities: list[dict]) -> None:\n   188→    \"\"\"Persist updated Transform components using PartiQL batch.\"\"\"\n   189→    statements = [\n   190→        {\n   191→            \"Statement\": (\n   192→                \"UPDATE \\\"{table}\\\" \"\n   193→                \"SET JSONPayload.pos = ? WHERE PartitionKey = ? AND SortKey = ?\"\n   194→            ).format(table=DYNAMO.table_name),\n   195→            \"Parameters\": [[Decimal(str(p)) for p in e[\"pos\"]], f\"TENANT#{tenant_id}\", f\"COMPONENT#Transform#{e['EntityId']}\"]\n   196→        }\n   197→        for e in entities\n   198→    ]\n   199→    DYNAMO.meta.client.batch_execute_statement(Statements=statements)\n   200→\n   201→def _send_to_dlq(event: dict, exc: Exception) -> None:\n   202→    \"\"\"Push failed message into SQS dead-letter queue.\"\"\"\n   203→    import boto3, json, os\n   204→    sqs = boto3.client(\"sqs\")\n   205→    sqs.send_message(\n   206→        QueueUrl=os.environ[\"DLQ_URL\"],\n   207→        MessageBody=json.dumps({\"event\": event, \"error\": str(exc)}),\n   208→    )\n   209→```\n   210→\n   211→---\n   212→\n   213→## 8. Error Handling & Observability\n   214→• **Retries**: Lambda is configured with max 2 retries; Step Functions use `Catch` with exponential backoff.  \n   215→• **DLQs**: Each System publishes into a tenant-scoped DLQ.  \n   216→• **Tracing**: AWS X-Ray auto-instrumentation via Lambda Powertools.  \n   217→• **Metrics**: Custom CloudWatch metrics (`FrameTime`, `PhysicsBodies`, `AIThinkTime`) feed into Game-SLA alarms.\n   218→\n   219→---\n   220→\n   221→## 9. Security & Compliance\n   222→1. **Multi-Tenant Key Isolation** — Every tenant has a dedicated KMS CMK; Envelope encryption for S3 assets.  \n   223→2. **Audit Trail** — Immutable, append-only log written by `Lambda_Audit` (backed by DynamoDB Streams + Timestream).  \n   224→3. **Fine-Grained IAM** — Inline policies generated per-tenant limiting data path to `TENANT#<id>*`.  \n   225→4. **Zero-Trust Networking** — No public subnets; Fargate tasks receive traffic only via PrivateLink from API GW.\n   226→\n   227→---\n   228→\n   229→## 10. Extensibility Points\n   230→• **Custom Systems** — Drop a Python module in `ledgerquest/systems`; register via CDK `SystemStack`.  \n   231→• **Behaviour Trees** — Author YAML or Behaviour3 JSON; deployed as S3 objects consumed by `Lambda_AI`.  \n   232→• **Scripting** — Python (restricted) or Lua executed in a `sandboxes-v2` Lambda layer with seccomp profile.\n   233→\n   234→---\n   235→\n   236→## 11. Deployment Pipeline (CI/CD)\n   237→\n   238→```yaml\n   239→# simplified .github/workflows/deploy.yml\n   240→name: Deploy LedgerQuest Engine\n   241→\n   242→on:\n   243→  push:\n   244→    branches: [ main ]\n   245→\n   246→jobs:\n   247→  test-and-deploy:\n   248→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n   249→    steps:\n   250→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4\n   251→      - name: Run unit tests\n   252→        run: |\n   253→          pip install -r requirements-dev.txt\n   254→          pytest\n   255→      - name: CDK Synth & Deploy\n   256→        env:\n   257→          AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: ${{ secrets.AWS_ID }}\n   258→          AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: ${{ secrets.AWS_SECRET }}\n   259→        run: |\n   260→          pip install -r infra/requirements.txt\n   261→          cdk synth\n   262→          cdk deploy --require-approval never\n   263→```\n   264→\n   265→---\n   266→\n   267→## 12. Architectural Tenets\n1. **Serverless–First**  \n   Every execution path is decomposed into fine-grained, stateless Lambdas, Step Functions, or Fargate tasks. This removes all **always-on** costs and yields “pay-for-play” economics.\n\n2. **Event-Driven Everything**  \n   Scene mutations, player inputs, AI ticks, and business telemetry ride the same EventBridge bus enabling loose-coupling, auditability, and near-infinite horizontal scale.\n\n3. **Enterprise-Grade Governance**  \n   Multi-tenant isolation, field-level encryption, and immutable audit logs are baked in through KMS, DynamoDB Streams, and CloudTrail integration—**without** sacrificing < 20 ms game-loop latency.\n\n4. **Pure ECS (Entity-Component-System)**  \n   Engine internals embrace a data-oriented ECS that maps cleanly onto distributed storage (DynamoDB) and compute (Lambda). Functional “Systems” are individually deployable as versioned Lambda layers.\n\n---\n\n## 2. High-Level System Diagram\n\n```mermaid\nflowchart TD\n    subgraph Client Tier\n        WebGL -->|WebSocket| APIGW_WS\n        Unity3D -->|REST/GraphQL| APIGW_HTTP\n    end\n\n    subgraph Control Plane\n        CI/CD --> CodePipeline --> {Canary Tests}\n    end\n\n    subgraph Game Plane\n        APIGW_WS --> Lambda_InputRouter\n        APIGW_HTTP --> Lambda_InputRouter\n        Lambda_InputRouter --> EventBridge\n        EventBridge -->|ai-tick| Lambda_AI\n        EventBridge -->|physics-tick| Lambda_Physics\n        EventBridge -->|scene-save| Lambda_Serializer\n        EventBridge --> StepFunction_GameLoop\n        StepFunction_GameLoop --> Lambda_AI\n        StepFunction_GameLoop --> Lambda_Physics\n        StepFunction_GameLoop --> Fargate_GPU\n    end\n\n    subgraph Data Plane\n        DynamoDB[(ECS Tables)]\n        S3[(Assets + Saves)]\n        Timestream[(Telemetry)]\n        DynamoDBStreams --> Lambda_Audit\n    end\n\n    Lambda_AI --> DynamoDB\n    Lambda_Physics --> DynamoDB\n    Lambda_Serializer --> S3\n    Fargate_GPU --> S3\n```\n\n---\n\n## 3. Layered Architecture\n\n1. **Presentation Layer**  \n   • Thin clients (React + Three.js, Unity, or native)  \n   • State updates via WebSockets; snapshots via pre-signed S3 URLs  \n\n2. **Orchestration Layer**  \n   • AWS Step Functions manage deterministic game loops (`≤ 60 Hz`)  \n   • Each Lambda/System is idempotent & side-effect-free outside DynamoDB  \n\n3. **Domain Layer**  \n   • ECS implementation (`Entity`, `Component`, `System`)  \n   • Command bus & Observer pattern enable scripting & modding  \n\n4. **Infrastructure Layer**  \n   • AWS CDK Type-Safe IaC (Python)  \n   • Multi-account, multi-region, blue-green deployments  \n\n---\n\n## 4. Component Responsibility Matrix\n\n| Concern                | Service / Pattern                  | Rationale                                    |\n|------------------------|------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|\n| Physics Simulation     | `Lambda_Physics` (+ Layers: PyBullet) | Deterministic, easily parallelisable         |\n| AI Behaviour Trees     | `Lambda_AI` (+ Layer: PyBehaviour) | Fine-grained per-Entity decision loops       |\n| Scripting System       | `Lambda_Scripting` (Python / Lua)  | Hot-swappable via Layer versioning           |\n| Rendering (Burst)      | `Fargate_GPU` (NV T4g)             | Short-lived cinematic renders & thumbnails   |\n| Level Editor           | `AppSync + Lambda_Graph`           | Real-time collaboration with CRDTs           |\n| Audit / Compliance     | `Lambda_Audit` + Timestream        | Immutable, query-ready logs                  |\n\n---\n\n## 5. Data Model (ECS Tables)\n\n```plaintext\nPartitionKey          SortKey               JSON Payload\n────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nTENANT#<id>           ENTITY#<uuid>         { \"components\": { ... } }\nTENANT#<id>           COMPONENT#Physics     { \"mass\": 1.2, ... }\nTENANT#<id>           COMPONENT#Transform   { \"pos\": [x,y,z], ... }\n```\n\n*Each component type is stored as a distinct item to maximise targeted updates and **avoid hot partitions**.*\n\n---\n\n## 6. Game Loop Orchestration (Step Functions)\n\n```mermaid\nstateDiagram-v2\n    [*] --> FrameStart\n    FrameStart --> PhysicsStep: Emit \"physics-tick\"\n    PhysicsStep --> AIStep: Emit \"ai-tick\"\n    AIStep --> RenderStep: Publish \"gpu-render\" (optional)\n    RenderStep --> SaveSnapshot: 1/60 probability\n    SaveSnapshot --> FrameEnd\n    FrameEnd --> [*]\n```\n\n• **Max Duration**: 16 ms per tick (60 FPS target)  \n• **Timeout Strategy**: Any System Lambda > 10 ms triggers circuit-breaker → degrade to 30 FPS for that tenant only.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Sample System Lambda (Physics)\n\n```python\n# file: ledgerquest/systems/physics.py\nimport json\nimport os\nfrom decimal import Decimal\nfrom typing import Dict, Any\n\nimport boto3\nimport pybullet as pb\nfrom botocore.config import Config\n\nDYNAMO = boto3.resource(\n    \"dynamodb\",\n    config=Config(retries={\"max_attempts\": 3, \"mode\": \"standard\"})\n).Table(os.environ[\"ECS_TABLE\"])\n\ndef lambda_handler(event: Dict[str, Any], _context) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Apply a single physics tick to the subset of entities that have\n    both Transform and Physics components.\n    `event` originates from EventBridge \"physics-tick\".\n    \"\"\"\n    tenant_id = event[\"detail\"][\"tenant_id\"]\n    try:\n        entities = _fetch_entities(tenant_id)\n        updated = _simulate(entities)\n        _persist(tenant_id, updated)\n        return {\"status\": \"ok\", \"count\": len(updated)}\n    except Exception as exc:            # noqa: BLE001\n        # Forward to DLQ for inspection; preserve idempotency by *not* re-raising\n        _send_to_dlq(event, exc)\n        return {\"status\": \"error\", \"message\": str(exc)}\n\ndef _fetch_entities(tenant_id: str) -> list[dict]:\n    \"\"\"Query DynamoDB for all Physics components within the current shard.\"\"\"\n    resp = DYNAMO.query(\n        KeyConditionExpression=\"PartitionKey = :tenant AND begins_with(SortKey, :prefix)\",\n        ExpressionAttributeValues={\":tenant\": f\"TENANT#{tenant_id}\", \":prefix\": \"COMPONENT#Physics\"},\n        ConsistentRead=False,\n    )\n    return resp[\"Items\"]\n\ndef _simulate(entities: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:\n    \"\"\"Run deterministic physics and return updated transforms.\"\"\"\n    pb.connect(pb.DIRECT)  # No GUI in Lambda\n    updated: list[dict] = []\n    for comp in entities:\n        e_id = comp[\"EntityId\"]\n        mass = float(comp[\"mass\"])\n        # Simplified example; real implementation would batch bodies\n        vel_x, vel_y, vel_z = 0.0, -9.81, 0.0\n        pos = comp[\"pos\"]\n        new_pos = [pos[0] + vel_x, pos[1] + vel_y * 0.016, pos[2] + vel_z]\n        updated.append({\"EntityId\": e_id, \"pos\": new_pos})\n    pb.disconnect()\n    return updated\n\ndef _persist(tenant_id: str, entities: list[dict]) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Persist updated Transform components using PartiQL batch.\"\"\"\n    statements = [\n        {\n            \"Statement\": (\n                \"UPDATE \\\"{table}\\\" \"\n                \"SET JSONPayload.pos = ? WHERE PartitionKey = ? AND SortKey = ?\"\n            ).format(table=DYNAMO.table_name),\n            \"Parameters\": [[Decimal(str(p)) for p in e[\"pos\"]], f\"TENANT#{tenant_id}\", f\"COMPONENT#Transform#{e['EntityId']}\"]\n        }\n        for e in entities\n    ]\n    DYNAMO.meta.client.batch_execute_statement(Statements=statements)\n\ndef _send_to_dlq(event: dict, exc: Exception) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Push failed message into SQS dead-letter queue.\"\"\"\n    import boto3, json, os\n    sqs = boto3.client(\"sqs\")\n    sqs.send_message(\n        QueueUrl=os.environ[\"DLQ_URL\"],\n        MessageBody=json.dumps({\"event\": event, \"error\": str(exc)}),\n    )\n```\n\n---\n\n## 8. Error Handling & Observability\n• **Retries**: Lambda is configured with max 2 retries; Step Functions use `Catch` with exponential backoff.  \n• **DLQs**: Each System publishes into a tenant-scoped DLQ.  \n• **Tracing**: AWS X-Ray auto-instrumentation via Lambda Powertools.  \n• **Metrics**: Custom CloudWatch metrics (`FrameTime`, `PhysicsBodies`, `AIThinkTime`) feed into Game-SLA alarms.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Security & Compliance\n1. **Multi-Tenant Key Isolation** — Every tenant has a dedicated KMS CMK; Envelope encryption for S3 assets.  \n2. **Audit Trail** — Immutable, append-only log written by `Lambda_Audit` (backed by DynamoDB Streams + Timestream).  \n3. **Fine-Grained IAM** — Inline policies generated per-tenant limiting data path to `TENANT#<id>*`.  \n4. **Zero-Trust Networking** — No public subnets; Fargate tasks receive traffic only via PrivateLink from API GW.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Extensibility Points\n• **Custom Systems** — Drop a Python module in `ledgerquest/systems`; register via CDK `SystemStack`.  \n• **Behaviour Trees** — Author YAML or Behaviour3 JSON; deployed as S3 objects consumed by `Lambda_AI`.  \n• **Scripting** — Python (restricted) or Lua executed in a `sandboxes-v2` Lambda layer with seccomp profile.\n\n---\n\n## 11. Deployment Pipeline (CI/CD)\n\n```yaml\n# simplified .github/workflows/deploy.yml\nname: Deploy LedgerQuest Engine\n\non:\n  push:\n    branches: [ main ]\n\njobs:\n  test-and-deploy:\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    steps:\n      - uses: actions/checkout@v4\n      - name: Run unit tests\n        run: |\n          pip install -r requirements-dev.txt\n          pytest\n      - name: CDK Synth & Deploy\n        env:\n          AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: ${{ secrets.AWS_ID }}\n          AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: ${{ secrets.AWS_SECRET }}\n        run: |\n          pip install -r infra/requirements.txt\n          cdk synth\n          cdk deploy --require-approval never\n```\n\n---\n\n## 12. Roadmap\n- [ ] Support for deterministic rollback (*lockstep*) multiplayer  \n- [ ] WASM-based scripting sandbox  \n- [ ] Machine-learning powered difficulty adjustment  \n- [ ] Cost-based optimiser for aggressive cold-start warming  \n\n---\n\n> © 2024 LedgerQuest Labs — Built with ❤️ for developers who believe business software can be **fun**.\n```","numLines":276,"startLine":1,"totalLines":276}}}

Context\n    18→\n    19→LedgerQuest Engine is tasked with delivering **real-time, multi-tenant gameplay** while meeting **business-grade** requirements such as cost-efficiency, compliance, and virtually unlimited scale.  \n    20→Traditional game loops are monolithic and heavily stateful, typically running at 30–144 FPS.  \n    21→Running such loops 24 × 7 across thousands of tenants would be **prohibitively expensive** on always-on compute.\n    22→\n    23→Key constraints:\n    24→\n    25→1. **Pay-per-request**: Idle costs must approach zero.\n    26→2. **Elastic latency**: Sub-250 ms E2E latency for interactive scenes.\n    27→3. **Deterministic state**: Authoritative state must be persisted for audit & replay.\n    28→4. **Composable**: Engine subsystems (physics, AI, networking) must evolve independently.\n    29→5. **Fault isolation**: A single tenant’s spike must not degrade others.\n    30→\n    31→After evaluating container-based and traditional dedicated servers, we concluded that **AWS Step Functions + Lambda** is the only approach satisfying all constraints.\n    32→\n    33→---\n    34→\n    35→## 2. Decision\n    36→\n    37→1. **Decompose** the canonical game loop into **stateless Lambda micro-ticks** orchestrated by **AWS Step Functions Standard Workflows**.  \n    38→   Each tick is < 900 ms, bounded by the Lambda invocation limit.\n    39→\n    40→2. **Persist** authoritative game state in **DynamoDB** using *single-table, multi-tenant* design with strict `tenant_id` partition keys + **record-level encryption**.\n    41→\n    42→3. **Emit** all side-effects through **Amazon EventBridge** for downstream consumers (audit logs, analytics, in-game notifications).\n    43→\n    44→4. **Overflow workloads** (GPU rendering, bulk physics) are off-loaded to **AWS Fargate** tasks triggered via **Step Functions `Map` states**.\n    45→\n    46→5. **WebSocket sessions** are managed by **API Gateway (WebSocket flavor)**. Clients send commands that enqueue a **StartExecution** call on a *per-session* Step Function.\n    47→\n    48→6. **Deterministic lock-free concurrency** is obtained using **DynamoDB conditional writes** (`ConditionExpression`) and **optimistic versioning**.\n    49→\n    50→---\n    51→\n    52→## 3. Detailed Mechanics\n    53→\n    54→### 3.1 State Machine Definition (Simplified)\n    55→\n    56→```python\n    57→# file: infra/game_loop_state_machine.py\n    58→import aws_cdk.aws_stepfunctions as sfn\n    59→import aws_cdk.aws_stepfunctions_tasks as tasks\n    60→from constructs import Construct\n    61→\n    62→class GameLoopStateMachine(Construct):\n    63→    \"\"\"\n    64→    Orchestrates a single tick of the serverless game loop.\n    65→    \"\"\"\n    66→    def __init__(self, scope: Construct, id_: str, *, table, physics_fn, ai_fn, persist_fn, **kwargs):\n    67→        super().__init__(scope, id_, **kwargs)\n    68→\n    69→        # 1. Load state\n    70→        load_state = tasks.DynamoGetItem(\n    71→            self, \"LoadState\",\n    72→            key={\"pk\": tasks.DynamoAttributeValue.from_string(sfn.JsonPath.string_at(\"$.tenant_id\"))},\n    73→            table=table,\n    74→            result_path=\"$.state\"\n    75→        )\n    76→\n    77→        # 2. Physics step\n    78→        physics_step = tasks.LambdaInvoke(\n    79→            self, \"PhysicsStep\",\n    80→            lambda_function=physics_fn,\n    81→            result_path=\"$.physics\"\n    82→        )\n    83→\n    84→        # 3. AI step\n    85→        ai_step = tasks.LambdaInvoke(\n    86→            self, \"AIStep\",\n    87→            lambda_function=ai_fn,\n    88→            result_path=\"$.ai\"\n    89→        )\n    90→\n    91→        # 4. Persist & Broadcast\n    92→        persist = tasks.LambdaInvoke(\n    93→            self, \"PersistAndBroadcast\",\n    94→            lambda_function=persist_fn,\n    95→            retry_on_service_exceptions=True,\n    96→            result_path=\"$.persist_result\"\n    97→        )\n    98→\n    99→        # Compose definition\n   100→        definition = (\n   101→            load_state\n   102→            .next(physics_step)\n   103→            .next(ai_step)\n   104→            .next(persist)\n   105→        )\n   106→\n   107→        self.state_machine = sfn.StateMachine(\n   108→            self, \"GameLoopSM\",\n   109→            definition=definition,\n   110→            timeout=core.Duration.seconds(10),   # generous for bursty steps\n   111→        )\n   112→```\n   113→\n   114→### 3.2 Activity Diagram\n   115→\n   116→```mermaid\n   117→stateDiagram-v2\n   118→    [*] --> LoadState\n   119→    LoadState --> PhysicsStep\n   120→    PhysicsStep --> AIStep\n   121→    AIStep --> PersistAndBroadcast\n   122→    PersistAndBroadcast --> [*]\n   123→```\n   124→\n   125→### 3.3 Lambda Micro-services\n   126→\n   127→All Lambda functions follow **hexagonal architecture**:\n   128→\n   129→```\n   130→├── handler.py\n   131→├── domain\n   132→│   ├── __init__.py\n   133→│   ├── physics.py\n   134→│   └── ai.py\n   135→├── infrastructure\n   136→│   ├── dynamodb_gateway.py\n   137→│   └── eventbridge_gateway.py\n   138→└── tests/\n   139→```\n   140→\n   141→They are **fully stateless**, receiving an *immutable* JSON event that includes:\n   142→\n   143→```json\n   144→{\n   145→  \"tenant_id\" : \"acme-corp\",\n   146→  \"frame_id\"  : 342987,\n   147→  \"command_batch\": [ ... ]\n   148→}\n   149→```\n   150→\n   151→Each function validates input via **Pydantic** models, then delegates to domain logic.  \n   152→Any unexpected exception is re-raised; Step Functions’ automatic retries & DLQs ensure **at-least-once** execution semantics.\n   153→\n   154→---\n   155→\n   156→## 4. Consequences\n   157→\n   158→Positive:\n   159→\n   160→* ✅ Idle cost ≈ 0. We pay only for executed frames.\n   161→* ✅ Horizontal scale is automatic—each step is naturally parallel across tenants.\n   162→* ✅ Audit compliance: every state mutation is a DynamoDB record & EventBridge event.\n   163→* ✅ Subsystems can be deployed independently (physics version `v2` can coexist with AI version `v5`).\n   164→\n   165→Negative / Trade-offs:\n   166→\n   167→* ❌ Cold-start latency. Mitigated via **provisioned concurrency** during peak hours.\n   168→* ❌ 900 ms hard limit for a micro-tick. Complex scenes must split over multiple ticks.\n   169→* ❌ Step Functions execution history impacts cost; we truncate history via `TTL`.\n   170→\n   171→---\n   172→\n   173→## 5. Alternatives Considered\n   174→\n   175→| Option                                      | Reason Rejected                                                 |\n   176→|---------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------|\n   177→| **“Always-on” ECS / Kubernetes fleet**      | Fails idle-cost constraint; heavier ops overhead.               |\n   178→| **Agones-based dedicated game servers**     | Optimised for fast-twitch games; not multi-tenant friendly.     |\n   179→| **Pure Lambda (no orchestrator)**           | Hard to manage complex error-handling, versioning, fan-out.     |\n   180→| **EventBridge Pipes without Step Functions**| Lacks step-level retries, wait-state semantics, & observability.|\n   181→\n   182→---\n   183→\n   184→## 6. Links\n   185→\n   186→* [AWS Docs – Step Functions Best Practices](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/step-functions/latest/dg/bp.html)  \n   187→* [ServerlessLand Pattern – WebSocket to Step Functions](https://serverlessland.com/patterns/apigw-websocket-stepfunctions)  \n   188→* [Gamasutra Post – “Deterministic Lock-Step in Cloud”](https://example.com/deterministic-lockstep)  \n   189→\n   190→---\n   191→\n   192→## 7. Decisions Revisited\n   193→\n   194→We will revisit this ADR when either:\n   195→\n   196→1. AWS releases **Lambda SnapStart for Python** (cold-start relief), or  \n   197→2.  > 20 % of game loops exceed the 900 ms limit, indicating fragmentation overhead.\n   198→\n   199→---\n   200→```\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. Context\n\nLedgerQuest Engine is tasked with delivering **real-time, multi-tenant gameplay** while meeting **business-grade** requirements such as cost-efficiency, compliance, and virtually unlimited scale.  \nTraditional game loops are monolithic and heavily stateful, typically running at 30–144 FPS.  \nRunning such loops 24 × 7 across thousands of tenants would be **prohibitively expensive** on always-on compute.\n\nKey constraints:\n\n1. **Pay-per-request**: Idle costs must approach zero.\n2. **Elastic latency**: Sub-250 ms E2E latency for interactive scenes.\n3. **Deterministic state**: Authoritative state must be persisted for audit & replay.\n4. **Composable**: Engine subsystems (physics, AI, networking) must evolve independently.\n5. **Fault isolation**: A single tenant’s spike must not degrade others.\n\nAfter evaluating container-based and traditional dedicated servers, we concluded that **AWS Step Functions + Lambda** is the only approach satisfying all constraints.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Decision\n\n1. **Decompose** the canonical game loop into **stateless Lambda micro-ticks** orchestrated by **AWS Step Functions Standard Workflows**.  \n   Each tick is < 900 ms, bounded by the Lambda invocation limit.\n\n2. **Persist** authoritative game state in **DynamoDB** using *single-table, multi-tenant* design with strict `tenant_id` partition keys + **record-level encryption**.\n\n3. **Emit** all side-effects through **Amazon EventBridge** for downstream consumers (audit logs, analytics, in-game notifications).\n\n4. **Overflow workloads** (GPU rendering, bulk physics) are off-loaded to **AWS Fargate** tasks triggered via **Step Functions `Map` states**.\n\n5. **WebSocket sessions** are managed by **API Gateway (WebSocket flavor)**. Clients send commands that enqueue a **StartExecution** call on a *per-session* Step Function.\n\n6. **Deterministic lock-free concurrency** is obtained using **DynamoDB conditional writes** (`ConditionExpression`) and **optimistic versioning**.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Detailed Mechanics\n\n### 3.1 State Machine Definition (Simplified)\n\n```python\n# file: infra/game_loop_state_machine.py\nimport aws_cdk.aws_stepfunctions as sfn\nimport aws_cdk.aws_stepfunctions_tasks as tasks\nfrom constructs import Construct\n\nclass GameLoopStateMachine(Construct):\n    \"\"\"\n    Orchestrates a single tick of the serverless game loop.\n    \"\"\"\n    def __init__(self, scope: Construct, id_: str, *, table, physics_fn, ai_fn, persist_fn, **kwargs):\n        super().__init__(scope, id_, **kwargs)\n\n        # 1. Load state\n        load_state = tasks.DynamoGetItem(\n            self, \"LoadState\",\n            key={\"pk\": tasks.DynamoAttributeValue.from_string(sfn.JsonPath.string_at(\"$.tenant_id\"))},\n            table=table,\n            result_path=\"$.state\"\n        )\n\n        # 2. Physics step\n        physics_step = tasks.LambdaInvoke(\n            self, \"PhysicsStep\",\n            lambda_function=physics_fn,\n            result_path=\"$.physics\"\n        )\n\n        # 3. AI step\n        ai_step = tasks.LambdaInvoke(\n            self, \"AIStep\",\n            lambda_function=ai_fn,\n            result_path=\"$.ai\"\n        )\n\n        # 4. Persist & Broadcast\n        persist = tasks.LambdaInvoke(\n            self, \"PersistAndBroadcast\",\n            lambda_function=persist_fn,\n            retry_on_service_exceptions=True,\n            result_path=\"$.persist_result\"\n        )\n\n        # Compose definition\n        definition = (\n            load_state\n            .next(physics_step)\n            .next(ai_step)\n            .next(persist)\n        )\n\n        self.state_machine = sfn.StateMachine(\n            self, \"GameLoopSM\",\n            definition=definition,\n            timeout=core.Duration.seconds(10),   # generous for bursty steps\n        )\n```\n\n### 3.2 Activity Diagram\n\n```mermaid\nstateDiagram-v2\n    [*] --> LoadState\n    LoadState --> PhysicsStep\n    PhysicsStep --> AIStep\n    AIStep --> PersistAndBroadcast\n    PersistAndBroadcast --> [*]\n```\n\n### 3.3 Lambda Micro-services\n\nAll Lambda functions follow **hexagonal architecture**:\n\n```\n├── handler.py\n├── domain\n│   ├── __init__.py\n│   ├── physics.py\n│   └── ai.py\n├── infrastructure\n│   ├── dynamodb_gateway.py\n│   └── eventbridge_gateway.py\n└── tests/\n```\n\nThey are **fully stateless**, receiving an *immutable* JSON event that includes:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"tenant_id\" : \"acme-corp\",\n  \"frame_id\"  : 342987,\n  \"command_batch\": [ ... ]\n}\n```\n\nEach function validates input via **Pydantic** models, then delegates to domain logic.  \nAny unexpected exception is re-raised; Step Functions’ automatic retries & DLQs ensure **at-least-once** execution semantics.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Consequences\n\nPositive:\n\n* ✅ Idle cost ≈ 0. We pay only for executed frames.\n* ✅ Horizontal scale is automatic—each step is naturally parallel across tenants.\n* ✅ Audit compliance: every state mutation is a DynamoDB record & EventBridge event.\n* ✅ Subsystems can be deployed independently (physics version `v2` can coexist with AI version `v5`).\n\nNegative / Trade-offs:\n\n* ❌ Cold-start latency. Mitigated via **provisioned concurrency** during peak hours.\n* ❌ 900 ms hard limit for a micro-tick. Complex scenes must split over multiple ticks.\n* ❌ Step Functions execution history impacts cost; we truncate history via `TTL`.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Alternatives Considered\n\n| Option                                      | Reason Rejected                                                 |\n|---------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------|\n| **“Always-on” ECS / Kubernetes fleet**      | Fails idle-cost constraint; heavier ops overhead.               |\n| **Agones-based dedicated game servers**     | Optimised for fast-twitch games; not multi-tenant friendly.     |\n| **Pure Lambda (no orchestrator)**           | Hard to manage complex error-handling, versioning, fan-out.     |\n| **EventBridge Pipes without Step Functions**| Lacks step-level retries, wait-state semantics, & observability.|\n\n---\n\n## 6. Links\n\n* [AWS Docs – Step Functions Best Practices](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/step-functions/latest/dg/bp.html)  \n* [ServerlessLand Pattern – WebSocket to Step Functions](https://serverlessland.com/patterns/apigw-websocket-stepfunctions)  \n* [Gamasutra Post – “Deterministic Lock-Step in Cloud”](https://example.com/deterministic-lockstep)  \n\n---\n\n## 7. Decisions Revisited\n\nWe will revisit this ADR when either:\n\n1. AWS releases **Lambda SnapStart for Python** (cold-start relief), or  \n2.  > 20 % of game loops exceed the 900 ms limit, indicating fragmentation overhead.\n\n---\n```","numLines":200,"startLine":1,"totalLines":200}}}

production-grade DynamoDB–backed implementation of the Entity–Component–System\npersistence layer used by LedgerQuest Engine.\n\nThis repository persists entities and their associated components into a\nsingle DynamoDB table using a composite primary key:\n\n    PK  = \"ENTITY#{tenant_id}#{entity_id}\"\n    SK  = \"COMPONENT#{component_name}\"\n\nThe design satisfies the following requirements:\n\n    • Multi-tenant isolation                (tenant_id included in the PK)\n    • Strongly-typed component marshaling   (dataclasses + Pydantic models)\n    • Auditable change history              (ConditionExpression + EventBridge)\n    • Optimistic concurrency control        (entity_version attribute)\n    • Incremental component loading         (query on PK, filter on SK)\n\nThe module is intentionally self-contained so that it can be imported by both\nLambda functions (per-request ECS mutations) and long-running game-loop\nsimulations scheduled in Fargate tasks.\n\nFunctions are synchronous for readability, but boto3 client calls are executed\nusing a ThreadPool so the interface remains non-blocking when used from\nasync/await code.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport logging\nimport os\nfrom concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor\nfrom dataclasses import asdict, dataclass, field, is_dataclass\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\nfrom functools import partial\nfrom typing import Any, Dict, Iterable, List, Mapping, Optional, Type, TypeVar\n\nimport boto3\nfrom botocore.exceptions import ClientError\nfrom pydantic import BaseModel, ValidationError\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n# Logging\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n_LOGGER = logging.getLogger(\"ledgerquest.ecs.dynamodb_repository\")\n_LOGGER.setLevel(logging.INFO)\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n# Exceptions\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n\nclass ECSRepositoryError(RuntimeError):\n    \"\"\"Base class for repository related failures.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass EntityNotFound(ECSRepositoryError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when an entity cannot be found in the database.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass ComponentValidationError(ECSRepositoryError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when a component payload fails validation.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass OptimisticLockError(ECSRepositoryError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when the entity_version no longer matches.\"\"\"\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n# Utility / Typing\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n_T = TypeVar(\"_T\", bound=\"ComponentModel\")\n\n\nclass ComponentModel(BaseModel):\n    \"\"\"Base class for any ECS Component stored in DynamoDB.\"\"\"\n\n    # Every component inherits tenant_id / entity_id so it can be reconstructed\n    tenant_id: str\n    entity_id: str\n\n    def pk(self) -> str:\n        return f\"ENTITY#{self.tenant_id}#{self.entity_id}\"\n\n    @property\n    def sk(self) -> str:\n        return f\"COMPONENT#{self.__class__.__name__}\"\n\n    class Config:\n        extra = --------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n    # Public API\n    # --------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n    def create_entity(\n        self, key: EntityKey, components: Iterable[ComponentModel]\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Insert a new entity; fails if the entity_id already exists for the\n        tenant. All components are stored in a single TransactWrite.\n\n        Raises:\n            ECSRepositoryError – on any DynamoDB failure\n            ComponentValidationError – if component fails validation\n        \"\"\"\n        timestamp = datetime.now(tz=timezone.utc).isoformat()\n        transact_items: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []\n\n        for component in components:\n            component_dict = _serialise_component(component, timestamp)\n            transact_items.append(\n                {\n                    \"Put\": {\n                        \"TableName\": self._table.name,\n                        \"Item\": component_dict,\n                        \"ConditionExpression\": \"attribute_not_exists(PK)\",\n                    }\n                }\n            )\n\n        # entity_version item\n        entity_meta = {\n            \"PK\": key.pk(),\n            \"SK\": \"META\",\n            \"entity_version\": 1,\n            \"created_at\": timestamp,\n            \"updated_at\": timestamp,\n        }\n        transact_items.append(\n            {\n                \"Put\": {\n                    \"TableName\": self._table.name,\n                    \"Item\": entity_meta,\n                    \"ConditionExpression\": \"attribute_not_exists(PK)\",\n                }\n            }\n        )\n\n        _LOGGER.debug(\"Creating entity %s with %d components\", key, len(components))\n        self._execute_transact_write(transact_items)\n        self._publish_event(\n            \"EntityCreated\", {\"tenantId\": key.tenant_id, \"entityId\": key.entity_id}\n        )\n\n    def get_components(\n        self,\n        key: EntityKey,\n        component_types: Optional[Iterable[Type[_T]]] = None,\n    ) -> Dict[str, _T]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Load components for an entity. If `component_types` is provided the query\n        will filter accordingly (client-side).\n\n        Raises:\n            EntityNotFound\n        \"\"\"\n        response = self._table.query(KeyConditionExpression=\"PK = :pk\", ExpressionAttributeValues={\":pk\": key.pk()})\n        if not response.get(\"Items\"):\n            raise EntityNotFound(f\"Entity {key} not found\")\n\n        components: Dict[str, _T] = {}\n        allowed_names = {c.__name__ for c in component_types} if component_types else None\n\n        for item in response[\"Items\"]:\n            if item[\"SK\"] == \"META\":\n                continue  # Verify entity exists\n        try:\n            self._table.get_item(Key={\"PK\": key.pk(), \"SK\": \"META\"})[\"Item\"]\n        except KeyError:\n            raise EntityNotFound(f\"Entity {key} not found\")\n\n        _LOGGER.debug(\"Deleting entity %s\", key)\n        statement = f\"DELETE FROM \\\"{self._table.name}\\\" WHERE PK=?\"\n        self._table.meta.client.execute_statement(Statement=statement, Parameters=[{\"S\": key.pk()}])\n        self._publish_event(\n            \"EntityDeleted\", {\"tenantId\": key.tenant_id, \"entityId\": key.entity_id}\n        )\n\n    # --------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n    # Internal helpers\n    # --------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n    def _execute_transact_write(self, items: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> None:\n        try:\n            future = self._executor.submit(\n                self._table.meta.client.transact_write_items, TransactItems=items\n            )\n            future.result()\n        except ClientError as exc:\n            _LOGGER.exception(\"DynamoDB TransactWrite failed: %s\", exc)\n            raise ECSRepositoryError(\"DynamoDB transact write failed\") from exc\n\n    def _publish_event(self, event_type: str, detail: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:\n        if not self._event_client:\n            return  # Fail silently – publishing is best-effort\n            _LOGGER.warning(\"EventBridge publish failed: %s\", exc)\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n# Component (De)Serialisation\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n\ndef _serialise_component(component: ComponentModel, timestamp: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n    if not isinstance(component, ComponentModel):\n        raise TypeError(\"component must inherit from ComponentModel\")\n    try:\n        payload = component.dict(by_alias=True)\n    except ValidationError as exc:\n        raise ComponentValidationError(str(exc)) from exc\n    return {\n        \"PK\": component.pk(),\n        \"SK\": component.sk,\n        \"data\": payload,\n        \"updated_at\": timestamp,\n    }\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n# Component Registry\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n_registry: Dict[str, Type[ComponentModel]] = {}\n\n\ndef register_component(cls: Type[_T]) -> Type[_T]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Class decorator registering a ComponentModel subclass for automatic\n    deserialisation. The decorated class *must* inherit from ComponentModel.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not issubclass(cls, ComponentModel):\n        raise TypeError(\"Only ComponentModel subclasses can be registered\")\n    _registry[cls.__name__] = cls\n    return cls\n\n\ndef _component_registry() -> Mapping[str, Type[ComponentModel]]:\n    return _registry\n\n\n# _is_conditional_failed(exc: ClientError) -> bool:\n    return (\n        exc.response.get(\"Error\", {}).get(\"Code\") == \"TransactionCanceledException\"\n        and any(\n            reason.get(\"Code\") == \"ConditionalCheckFailed\"\n            for reason in exc.response.get(\"CancellationReasons\", [])\n        )\n    )\n\n\n# The state machine orchestrates\neach frame-tick by invoking small, stateless Lambda functions that operate on\nexternalised entity data (DynamoDB, S3, etc.).  The design embraces the classic\ngame-loop phases—input, AI, physics, rendering, and persistence—while staying\ncompletely serverless and horizontally scalable.\n\nWhy is this file a *.asl.json in source control but a Python module here?\n---------------------------------------------------------------------------\nThe CI pipeline expects a pure ASL JSON file in `infra/step_functions`, but\nauthoring complex ASL by hand is error-prone.  This Python module generates the\nsame JSON deterministically, enables compile-time linting, strong typing, and\nunit tests, and can even bootstrap/deploy the state machine via `boto3`.\n\nRunning the module directly will:\n    1. Emit the ASL JSON to the canonical path expected by CDK/Terraform.\n    2. Optionally create/update the Step Function in the target AWS account\n       when the `--deploy` flag is provided.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport botocore.exceptions import BotoCoreError, ClientError\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n# Configuration dataclass                                                     #\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass LambdaArns:\n    \"\"\"Fully-qualified ARNs for the Lambda GameLoopStateMachineConfig:\n    \"\"\"\n    All inputs required to generate and/or deploy the state machine definition.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    lambda_arns: LambdaArns\n    role_arn: str  # IAM role #\n# State machine builder                                                       #\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n\ndef _seconds_per_tick(tick_rate_hz: int) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Small helper to convert a tick rate to a Step Functions-compatible duration.\"\"\"\n    return max(1.0 / tick_rate_hz, 0.001)  # SFN minimum is 0.001 seconds\n\n\ndef build_state_machine(cfg: GameLoopStateMachineConfig) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Return the Amazon States Language (ASL) document for the LedgerQuest game loop.\n\n    The state machine is intentionally linear and deterministic to guarantee\n    strict ordering of mutations against entity state in DynamoDB.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Shorthand for readability\n    lbd = \"InitializeFrame\",\n        \"Version\": \"1.0\",\n        \"TimeoutSeconds\": 60,\n        \"States\": {\n            \"InitializeFrame\": {\n                \"Type\": \"Pass\",\n                \"ResultPath\": \"$.metadata\",\n                \"Result\": {\n                    \"frameStartEpochMs.$\": \"$$.State.EnteredTime\",\n                    \"tickRateHz\": cfg.tick_rate_hz,\n                },\n                \"Next\": \"ProcessInput\",\n            },\n            \"ProcessInput\": _lambda_task_state(\n                name=\"ProcessInput\",\n                function_arn=lbd.process_input,\n                next_state=\"UpdateAI\",\n            ),\n            \"UpdateAI\": _lambda_task_state(\n                name=\"UpdateAI\",\n                function_arn=lbd.update_ai,\n                next_state=\"PhysicsStep\",\n            ),\n            \"PhysicsStep\": _lambda_task_state(\n                name=\"PhysicsStep\",\n                function_arn=lbd.physics_step,\n                next_state=\"RenderFrame\",\n            ),\n            \"RenderFrame\": _lambda_task_state(\n                name=\"RenderFrame\",\n                function_arn=lbd.render_frame,\n                next_state=\"PersistState\",\n            ),\n            \"PersistState\": _lambda_task_state(\n                name=\"PersistState\",\n                function_arn=lbd.persist_state,\n                next_state=\"WaitForNextTick\",\n            ),\n            \"WaitForNextTick\": {\n                \"Type\": \"Wait\",\n                \"Seconds\": seconds_per_tick,\n                \"Next\": \"LoopDecider\",\n            },\n            \"LoopDecider\": {\n                \"Type\": \"Choice\",\n                \"Choices\": [\n                    {\n                        \"Variable\": \"$.continue\",\n                        \"BooleanEquals\": True,\n                        \"Next\": \"InitializeFrame\",\n                    }\n                ],\n                \"Default\": \"GameFinished\",\n            },\n            \"GameFinished\": {\n                \"Type\": \"Succeed\",\n                \"Comment\": \"Game ended normally.\",\n            },\n        },\n    }\n    return definition\n\n\ndef _lambda_task_state(\n    *, name: str, function_arn: str, next_state: str\n) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"\n    DRY helper to create a Lambda task state with common error handling and\n    exponential back-off strategy.\n    \"\"\"\n    return {\n        \"Type\": \"Task\",\n        \"Resource\": \"arn:aws:states:::lambda:invoke\",\n    \"ErrorEquals\": [\"Lambda.ServiceException\", \"Lambda.AWSLambdaException\"],\n                \"IntervalSeconds\": 2,\n                \"MaxAttempts\": 6,\n                \"BackoffRate\": 2.0,\n \"GameFinished\",\n            }\n        ],\n        \"Next\": next_state,\n    }\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n# JSON emission & deployment helpers                                          #\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n\ndef write_asl(cfg: GameLoopStateMachineConfig, definition: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Persist the generated ASL to disk (pretty-printed and sorted).\"\"\"\n    cfg.output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    with configured via environment variables, instance\n    profile, or ~/.aws/credentials.\n    \"\"\"\n    client = boto3.client(\"stepfunctions\")\n\n    # Attempt to find an existing state machine by ClientError) as ex:\n        logging.error(\"Unable to update Step Function: %s\", ex)\n        raise SystemExit(2)\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- argparse.Namespace:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\"Generate & deploy LedgerQuest game-loop state machine.\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--process-input-arn\", required=True, help=\"Lambda ARN for the input-processing task\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--update-ai-arn\", required=True, help=\"Lambda ARN for the AI update task\")\n parser.add_argument(\"--deploy\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"Create/Update the state machine in AWS\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"-v\", \"--verbose\", action=\"count\", default=0)\n    return The processor also performs tenant-aware\nvalidation, idempotency checks, and finally publishes the commands to\nan internal EventBridge bus where downstream stateless engines (AI,\nphysics, etc.) will pick the items up.\n\nCore Responsibilities\n---------------------\n1. Command translation (Command Pattern).\n4. Emission of validated commands to AWS EventBridge with the proper\n   routing keys so that subscriber Lambdas / Step-Function workflows\n   can continue the game loop.\n\nThe module purposefully keeps Guiding Principles\n    17→\n    18→| Principle                         | Implementation Hint                                                                                               |\n    19→| -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n    20→| Single Table per Bounded Context | We use **single-table design** for each core domain (`core-ecs`, `core-player`, `core-billing`).                   |\n    21→| Append-only for Compliance       | Mutations emit **LedgerEvents** (WORM) stored in S3 Glacier + DynamoDB Streams before the actual update commits. |\n    22→| Tenant Isolation                 | Every PK is prefixed with the **Tenant UUID**; IAM policies enforce boundary checks at the table level.           |\n    23→| Game Loop Performance            | Time-critical lookups (e.g., component hydration) leverage **DAX** and **in-memory caches** inside warm Lambdas.  |\n    24→| Auditable & Replayable           | The **EventStore** acts as the single source of truth to rebuild every game state exactly as it unfolded.         |\n    25→\n    26→---\n    27→\n    28→## 2. Entity Overview\n    29→\n    30→```mermaid\n    31→erDiagram\n    32→    TENANT ||--o{ USER           : owns\n    33→    TENANT ||--o{ GAME_SESSION   : owns\n    34→    USER   ||--o{ GAME_SESSION   : participates\n    35→    GAME_SESSION ||--o{ ECS_ENTITY  : spawns\n    36→    ECS_ENTITY ||--o{ COMPONENT   : composed_of\n    37→```\n    38→\n    39→* **Tenant** – SaaS customer isolation boundary.  \n    40→* **User** – Player or admin within a tenant.  \n    41→* **GameSession** – A single play-through instance.  \n    42→* **EcsEntity / Component** – Classic ECS modelling.  \n    43→* **LedgerEvent** – Immutable event record for every state change (audit log + replay).\n    44→\n    45→---\n    46→\n    47→## 3. DynamoDB Schemas\n    48→\n    49→### 3.1 `core-tenant` Table\n    50→\n    51→| Key                 | Type | Example                          | Notes                                         |\n    52→| ------------------- | ---- | -------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- |\n    53→| **PK**              | S    | `TENANT#0f513bb6`               | Tenant-scoped                                 |\n    54→| **SK**              | S    | `METADATA`                       | Constant; single-item entity                  |\n    55→| `plan`              | S    | `enterprise`                     | Billing tier                                  |\n    56→| `created_at`        | N    | `1686282148`                     | Unix epoch                                    |\n    57→| `is_suspended`      | BOOL | `false`                          | Flag set by billing micro-service             |\n    58→| **GSI1PK / GSI1SK** | —    | —                                | Not required                                  |\n    59→\n    60→### 3.2 `core-player` Table (Single-Table Design)\n    61→\n    62→| PK Example                       | SK Example Guiding Principles\n\n| Principle                         | Implementation Hint                                                                                               |\n| -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n| Single Table per Bounded Context | We use **single-table design** for each core domain (`core-ecs`, `core-player`, `core-billing`).                   |\n| Append-only for Compliance       | Mutations emit **LedgerEvents** (WORM) stored in S3 Glacier + DynamoDB Streams before the actual update commits. |\n| Tenant Isolation                 | Every PK is prefixed with the **Tenant UUID**; IAM policies enforce boundary checks at the table level.           |\n| Game Loop Performance            | Time-critical lookups (e.g., component hydration) leverage **DAX** and **in-memory caches** inside warm Lambdas.  |\n| Auditable & Replayable           | The **EventStore** acts as the single source of truth to rebuild every game state exactly as it unfolded.         |\n\n---\n\n## 2. Entity Overview\n\n```mermaid\nerDiagram\n    TENANT ||--o{ USER           : owns\n    TENANT ||--o{ GAME_SESSION   : owns\n    USER   ||--o{ GAME_SESSION   : participates\n    GAME_SESSION ||--o{ ECS_ENTITY  : spawns\n    ECS_ENTITY ||--o{ COMPONENT   : composed_of\n```\n\n* **Tenant** – SaaS customer isolation boundary.  \n* **User** – Player or admin within a tenant.  \n* **GameSession** – A single play-through instance.  \n* **EcsEntity / Component** – Classic ECS modelling.  \n* **LedgerEvent** – Immutable event record for every state change (audit log + replay).\n\n---\n\n## 3. DynamoDB Schemas\n\n### 3.1 `core-tenant` Table\n\n| Key                 | Type | Example                          | Notes                                         |\n| ------------------- | ---- | -------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- |\n| **PK**              | S    | `TENANT#0f513bb6`               | Tenant-scoped                                 |\n| **SK**              | S    | `METADATA`                       | Constant; single-item entity                  |\n| `plan`              | S    | `enterprise`                     | Billing tier                                  |\n| `created_at`        | N    | `1686282148`                     | Unix epoch                                    |\n| `is_suspended`      | BOOL | `false`                          | Flag set by billing micro-service             |\n| **GSI1PK / GSI1SK** | —    | —                                | Not required                                  |\n\n### 3.2 `core-player` Table (Single-Table Design)\n\n| PK Example                       | SK Example                            | Entity Type   |\n| -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------- | ------------- |\n| `TENANT#0f513bb6`                | `USER#12ac59e9`                       | User          |\n| `TENANT#0f513bb6`                | `SESSION#2024-05-01T12:04:16Z`        | GameSession   |\n| `TENANT#0f513bb6`                | `SESSION#...#PLAYER#12ac59e9`         | Player link   |\n\nExtras:\n\n* **GSI1** – `GSI1PK = USER#{UserId}`, `GSI1SK = SESSION#{SessionId}`  \n  Allows “my sessions” queries.\n\n* **Streams** – `NEW_IMAGE` routed to the Audit-Lambda (→ EventStore).\n\n### 3.3 `core-ecs` Table\n\nPartition strategy that prevents hot partitions when thousands of entities spawn per tick:\n\n```text\nPK  = TENANT#{TenantId}#S#{SessionId}\nSK  = E#{hash(EntityId)}#{EntityId}    -- hashed prefix randomises access\n```\n\nAttributes:\n\n* `components` – Map where key = `component_name`, value = blob (zstd-compressed JSON).\n* `version` – Optimistic lock counter (CAS writes via `ConditionExpression`).\n* `ttl` – Optional (only transient entities).\n\n### 3.4 `event-store` Table (Append-Only)\n\n| Attribute    | Value (example)                                   |\n| ------------ | ------------------------------------------------- |\n| PK           | `TENANT#0f513bb6`                                 |\n| SK           | `2024-05-01T12:04:17.017Z#SESSION_CREATED`        |\n| `payload`    | JSON (opaque)                                     |\n| `checksum`   | SHA-256 of payload                                |\n| `author`     | `USER#12ac59e9`                                   |\n| `replay_ptr` | S3 object key for large payload (>350 KB)         |\n\nTTL is disabled—compliance rules require 7-year retention.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Object Storage (S3)\n\n```text\nledgerquest-{env}-{region}-{account}/\n├── tenants/{tenant_id}/\n│   ├── assets/{hash}.{ext}\n│   ├── events/2024/05/...\n│   └── exports/{session_id}/checkpoint-{tick}.json\n└── global/\n    └── marketplace/...\n```\n\n* **Asset Fingerprinting** – Content-addressable (SHA-1) keys to dedupe uploads.  \n* **Versioned Buckets** – Enables rollback during incident response.  \n* **Lifecycle** – Raw event objects transition → Glacier after 30 days.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Typical Write Path\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    participant Client\n    participant API as API Gateway\n    participant Cmd as Command-Lambda\n    participant Audit as Audit-Lambda\n    participant DB as DynamoDB\n    participant Bus as EventBridge\n\n    Client-->>API: POST /startSession\n    API-->>Cmd: Lambda Invoke (authorised)\n    Cmd-->>Audit: PutEvent (LedgerEvent: SESSION_CREATED)\n    Audit-->>DB: PutItem(event-store)\n    Cmd-->>DB: TransactWrite(core-player + core-ecs)\n    Cmd-->>Bus: PutEvents(GameSessionStarted)\n    API-->>Client: 200 OK + SessionId\n```\n\nWrite latency budget:\n\n* **P95 ≤ 45 ms** for interactive flows  \n* **P99 ≤ 100 ms** for bulk/async writes (e.g., level editing)\n\n---\n\n## 6. Example Items\n\n### 6.1 Game Session\n\n```jsonc\n{\n  \"PK\": \"TENANT#0f513bb6\",\n  \"SK\": \"SESSION#2024-05-01T12:04:16Z\",\n  \"session_id\": \"2024-05-01T12:04:16Z\",\n  \"status\": \"RUNNING\",\n  \"seed\": 91356012,\n  \"config_hash\": \"sha1:84dd8c3d3...\",\n  \"created_at\": 1714560256,\n  \"expires_at\": 1717162256  // TTL (optional)\n}\n```\n\n### 6.2 ECS Entity with Components\n\n```jsonc\n{\n  \"PK\": \"TENANT#0f513bb6#S#2024-05-01T12:04:16Z\",\n  \"SK\": \"E#83a7c32e#abc123\",\n  \"entity_id\": \"abc123\",\n  \"components\": {\n    \"Transform\": \"eJyLjgUAARUAuQ==\",  // zstd-base64\n    \"RigidBody\": \"eJyrVkrLz1eyUkpKLFKqBQA1xgPc\"\n  },\n  \"version\": 27\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 7. Concurrency & Consistency\n\n1. Optimistic locking (DynamoDB `version` attribute + `ConditionExpression`)  \n2. Idempotent command IDs to swallow client retries  \n3. Saga orchestration for multi-item commits spanning tables/buckets\n\nFailure modes:\n\n* **Audit write fails** → Primary mutation is aborted (`TransactWrite`)  \n* **Downstream consumer lag** → Kinesis DLQ retains change until re-processed  \n* **Hot partition** → Key hashing + adaptive **write-sharding** (power-of-two choices)\n\n---\n\n## 8. Security & Compliance\n\n* **Encryption at rest** – AWS-managed KMS keys (`alias/ledgerquest/*`)  \n* **Row-level isolation** – IAM Condition `\"dynamodb:LeadingKeys\" == ${aws:PrincipalTag/TenantId}`  \n* **Field-level encryption** – PII (email) encrypted client-side using XChaCha20-Poly1305  \n* **Audit immutability** – EventStore + S3 set to `compliance-mode` WORM\n\n---\n\n## 9. Change Log\n\n| Version | Date       | Author         | Notes                             |\n| ------- | ---------- | -------------- | --------------------------------- |\n| 1.3.0   | 2024-06-09 | @core-team     | Added DAX mention & write budget. |\n| 1.2.0   | 2024-05-18 | @sec-ops       | Field-level encryption section.   |\n| 1.0.0   | 2024-04-01 | @arch-leads    | Initial draft.                    |\n\n---\n\n> Need a new attribute or table? Create a PR targeting `/docs/architecture/data_model.md` and involve @data-gov for schema review.\n\n```","numLines":219,"startLine":1,"totalLines":219}}}

The module provides an opinionated wrapper around\nDynamoDB for low-latency, per-tick mutations (optimistic-locking included)\nplus a snapshotter that durably archives every committed revision to S3.\n\nBoth services are discovered via environment variables so that the same code\nruns unmodified in local development (e.g. with `localstack`) and AWS Lambda.\n\nEnvironment variables\n---------------------\nLEDGERQUEST_GAMESTATE_DDB_TABLE   DynamoDB table storing the latest document\nLEDGERQUEST_GAMESTATE_S3_BUCKET   S3 bucket used for *immutable* snapshots\nLEDGERQUEST_AWS_REGION            If absent, falls back to AWS_DEFAULT_REGION\n\nThe document schema stored in DynamoDB looks like this:\n\n    PK              :  <TENANT>#<GAME_ID>\n    SK              :  \"STATE\"\n    version         :  int  (monotonically increasing, starts at 1)\n    updated_at      :  int  (unix epoch millis)\n    payload         :  map (arbitrary JSON–serialisable object)\n\nThe table should have `PK` as the partition key and `SK` as the sort key.\n`version` is used for conditional writes to implement optimistic locking.\n\nSnapshot objects in S3 are written to:\n\n    s3://{bucket}/{tenant_id}/{game_id}/{version}.json\n\n…where the file contents are the exact `payload` document plus a small\nmetadata header (see :pyfunc:`_build_snapshot_blob`).\n\nNote\n----\nThis module intentionally avoids any notion of “transient / in-memory”\ngame-loop data. _Deserializer:\n        _deserializers[type_name] = func\n        logger.debug(\"Registered deserializer for %s: %s\", type_name, func)\n        return func\n\n    return decorator\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n# Public helpers\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\ndef dumps(\n    obj: Any,\n    *,\n    fmt: SerializationFormat = SerializationFormat.JSON,\n    compress: bool | int = False,\n    encryption_key: bytes | None = None,\n    metadata: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n) -> bytes:\n    \"\"\"\n    Serialise *obj* according to *fmt* and return raw bytes suitable for\n    DynamoDB/S3.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    obj:\n        The Python object to serialise.\n    fmt:\n        Wire format.  ``json`` has broader tool support, while ``msgpack`` is\n        more bandwidth-efficient (but optional).\n    compress:\n        ``False``: no compression. ``True``: Requires\n        ``pip install cryptography``.\n    metadata:\n        Additional key/value pairs to embed in the *header* (e.g. game/session\n        identifiers). It is designed to\nbe triggered by an AWS Step-Functions `Task` state (or EventBridge\nScheduler), and persists the updated entity components back to\nDynamoDB.  The routine is intentionally lightweight—only the minimal\nmath required for business-grade “serious games”.  Heavy GPU/CPU\nworkloads should still be off-loaded to dedicated workers (e.g.\nFargate).\n\nEnvironment variables\n---------------------\nENTITIES_TABLE_NAME   : DynamoDB table that stores entity component state\nEVENT_BUS_NAME        : Optional ‑ EventBridge bus to publish collision events\nLOG_LEVEL             : stdlib logging level (default=INFO)\n\nInput event contract\n--------------------\n{\n    \"scene_id\": \"<uuid>\",\n    \"delta_time\": 0.016  # half-width / half-height (extents)\n    mass: float\n    is_static: bool = False\n    version: int = 0  # optimistic-locking version\n\n    # runtime-only computed field (not persisted)\n    _aabb: AABB = field(init=False, repr=False)\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        self.compute_aabb()\n\n    # It's still\n            # acceptable for small partitions (< ~100 items).\n            logger.debug(\"Falling back to full scan for scene_id=%s\", self.scene_id)\n            response = _entities_table.scan(\n                FilterExpression=\"pk = :pk AND component = :physics\",\n                ExpressionAttributeValues={\n                    \":pk\": f\"scene#{self.scene_id}\",\n                    \":physics\": \"physics\",\n                }\n            )\n\n        items = response.get(\"Items\", [])\n        self.entities = [EntityPhysicsState.from_dynamo_item(i) for i in items]\n\n    def _apply_forces(self) -> None:\n        for ent in self.entities:\n            ent.apply_gravity(self.delta)\n\n    def _integrate(self) -> None:\n        for ent in self.entities:\n            ent.integrate(self.delta)\n\n    def _detect_and_resolve_collisions(self) -> None:\n        # Naïve O(N^2) for demo purposes; optimise using spatial hashing if needed\n        count = len(self.entities)\n        for i in range(count):\n            a = self.entities[i]\n            if a.is_static:\n                continue\n            for j in range(i + 1, count):\n                b = self.entities[j]\n                if a._aabb.intersects(b._aabb):\n                    # Simple symmetric collision response\n                    normal = self._compute_collision_normal(a, b)\n                    a.bounce(normal)\n                    if not b.is_static:\n                        b.bounce((-normal[0], -normal[1]))\n                    self.collisions.append((a.entity_id, b.entity_id))\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _compute_collision_normal(a: EntityPhysicsState, b: EntityPhysicsState) -> Tuple[float, float]:\n        # Determine which axis penetration is smaller\n        dx = (b.pos.x - a.pos.x)\n        dy = (b.pos.y - a.pos.y)\n        abs_dx, abs_dy = abs(dx), abs(dy)\n        if abs_dx > abs_dy:\n            # collision comes from left or right\n            return (1 if dx > 0 else -1, 0)\n        else:\n            return (0, 1 if dy > 0 else -1)\n\n    def _persist(self) -> None:\n        # limit to 10 per invocation\n            entries.append(\n                {\n                    \"Source\": \"ledgerquest.physics\",\n                    \"DetailType\": \"COLLISION\",\n                    \"EventBusName\": _EVENT_BUS_NAME,\n                    \"Time\": int(time.time() * 1000),\n                    \"Detail\": json.dumps(\n                        {\n                            \"scene_id\": self.scene_id,\n                            \"a\": a_id,\n                            \"b\": b_id,\n                        }\n                    ),\n                }\n            )\n\n        # Expects a Step-Function / EventBridge payload.\n\n    Parameter\n    ---------\n    event : dict\n        {\n            \"scene_id\": \"<uuid>\",\n            \"delta_time\": 0.016\n        }\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    dict  # same shape for downstream steps\n    \"\"\"\n    t0 = time.perf_counter()\n    try:\n        scene_id = str(event[\"scene_id\"])\n        delta = float(event.get(\"delta_time\", 0.016))\n    except (KeyError, ValueError, TypeError) as exc:\n        logger.error(\"Bad input event: %s\", exc, exc_info=True)\n        raise\n\n    logger.info(\"Physics step start scene=%s  Δt=%.4fs\", scene_id, delta)\n\n    simulator = PhysicsUpdater(scene_id, delta)\n    simulator.step()\n\n    duration_ms = (time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1000.0\n    logger.info(\n        \"Physics step complete scene=%s entities=%d collisions=%d (%.2fms)\",\n        scene_id,\n        len(simulator.entities),\n        len(simulator.collisions),\n        duration_ms,\n    )\n\n    return {\n        \"scene_id\": scene_id,\n        \"updated_entities\": len(simulator.entities),\n        \"collisions\": len(simulator.collisions),\n        \"duration_ms\": round(duration_ms, 2),\n    }\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------#\n# Utility helpers\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------#\ndef _to_dynamo_float(value: float) -> Decimal:\n    \"\"\"\n    DynamoDB does not support IEEE-754 floats; we use `Decimal` with a fixed\n    precision to maintain deterministic JSON hashing across our pipeline.\n    \"\"\"\n    fmt = f\"{{:.{DYNAMO_FLOAT_PRECISION}f}}\"\n    return Decimal(fmt.format(value))\n","numLines":421,"startLine":1,"totalLines":421}}}

The function is invoked by an AWS Step-Functions state machine\nthat orchestrates the stateless “game-loop” for LedgerQuest Engine.\n\nThe AI updater performs the following high-level steps:\n\n1. Command – an immutable, serialisable value-object representing an\n   intent to change game state.\n2. CommandHandler – an abstract base for classes that *execute* a\n   Command against the external ECS/DynamoDB/S3 back-end.\n3. CommandRegistry – a lightweight service-locator that maps Command\n   types to their respective handlers.  Registration happens\n   automatically through decorators.\n4. CommandBus – a thin façade that receives a Command instance,\n   resolves the correct handler, and calls `handle()` with robust\n   error-handling and audit logging.\n\nThese building blocks are intentionally framework-agnostic so they can\nbe used inside AWS Lambda functions, Step-Functions state machines, or\nlocal unit tests with equal ease.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import \"CommandHandler\",\n    \"CommandRegistry\",\n    \"CommandBus\",\n    \"CommandError\",\n    \"CommandValidationError\",\n    \"CommandHandlerNotFound\",\n    \"ECSGateway\",\n]\n\nlog =            #\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n\nclass CommandError(RuntimeError):\n    \"\"\"Base-class for all Command-related \"\"\"Raised when the CommandBus cannot locate a handler for the given Command.\"\"\"\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n#                   Core Domain – Command & Handler                           #\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\nT_Command = TypeVar(\"T_Command\", Concrete command classes should inherit from this base\n    class and declare their own business fields:\n\n        @command\n        @dataclass(frozen=True, kw_only=True)\n        class SpawnNPC(Command):\n            npc_type: str\n            position: Vec3\n            tenant_id: str\n            user_id: str\n    \"\"\"\n\n    tenant_id: str\n    user_id: str\n    correlation_id: Optional[str] = None\n\n    # Metadata automatically injected for traceability.\n    command_id: str = field(default_factory=lambda: str(uuid.uuid4()))\n    created_at: float = field(default_factory=lambda: time.time())\n\n    # ------------------------- Convenience helpers ------------------------ #\n\n    def iso_timestamp(self) -> str:\n        return (\n            datetime.fromtimestamp(self.created_at, tz=timezone.utc)\n            .replace(microsecond=0)\n            .isoformat()\n        )\n\n    def as_dict(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n        return asdict(self)\n\n    def to_json(self) -> str:\n        return json.dumps(self.as_dict(), default=str, separators=(\",\", \":\"))\n\n\nclass ECSGateway(ABC):\n    \"\"\"\n    Very thin Repository/DAO abstraction for the ECS back-end\n    (DynamoDB, S3, Redis, etc.).
