Handlers must be\n    stateless and thread-safe; any shared dependencies (ECS gateway,\n    loggers, etc.) are injected at construction time.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # Derived classes should set this to the *Command* subclass handled.\n    command_cls: Type[T_Command]\n\n    def __init__(self, *, ecs_gateway: ECSGateway, logger: Optional[logging.Logger] = None) -> None:\n        self._ecs_gateway = ecs_gateway\n        self._logger = logger or logging.getLogger(self.__class__.__name__)\n        # Defensive: ensure subclass configured command_cls.\n        if not hasattr(self, \"command_cls\") or not inspect.isclass(self.command_cls):\n            raise TypeError(\n                f\"{self.__class__.__name__} must set `command_cls` attribute \"\n                \"pointing to the Command it handles.\"\n            )\n\n    # pylint: disable=unused-argument\n    def validate(self, command: T_Command) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Optional validation hook.  Sub-classes should raise\n        CommandValidationError for invalid payloads.\n        \"\"\"\n\n    @abstractmethod\n    def handle(self, command: T_Command) -> Any:  # noqa: D401\n        \"\"\"\n        Execute the given Command.  Should return a serialisable result\n        (dict, list, str, etc.) that can be included in Lambda/HTTP\n        responses or Step-Functions state outputs.\n        \"\"\"\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n#               Service-Locator – Register & Discover handlers                #\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n\nclass CommandRegistry:\n    \"\"\"\n    A global registry mapping Command subclasses to their respective\n    *handler* classes.  The registry is intentionally simple and\n    depends on explicit code-based registration to avoid import-time\n    magic that can misbehave in Lambda’s frozen environments.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    _command_to_handler: MutableMapping[Type[Command], Type[CommandHandler]] = {}\n\n    # ------------------------------ API ----------------------------------- #\n\n    @classmethod\n    def register(\n        cls, *, command_cls: Type[Command], handler_cls: Type[CommandHandler]\n    ) -> None:\n        log.debug(\n            \"Registering Command handler – %s -> %s\",\n            command_cls.__qualname__,\n            handler_cls.__qualname__,\n        )\n        if command_cls in cls._command_to_handler:\n            existing = cls._command_to_handler[command_cls]\n            raise RuntimeError(\n                f\"Duplicate handler for {command_cls}: \"\n                f\"{existing.__qualname__} vs {handler_cls.__qualname__}\"\n            )\n        cls._command_to_handler[command_cls] = handler_cls\n\n    @classmethod\n    def handler_for(cls, command_cls: Type[T_Command]) -> Optional[Type[CommandHandler[T_Command]]]:\n        return cls._command_to_handler.get(command_cls)\n\n    # --------------------------- Decorators ------------------------------- #\n\n    @classmethod\n    def command_handler(\n        cls, command_cls: Type[T_Command]\n    ):  # noqa: D401  # non-standard decorator signature\n        \"\"\"\n        Decorator for registering a CommandHandler.\n\n            @CommandRegistry.command_handler(MyCommand)\n            class MyCommandHandler(CommandHandler[MyCommand]):\n                ...\n        \"\"\"\n\n        def _decorator(handler_cls: Type[CommandHandler[T_Command]]) -> Type[CommandHandler]:\n            cls.register(command_cls=command_cls, handler_cls=handler_cls)\n            return handler_cls\n\n        return _decorator\n\n    # --------------------------- Discovery -------------------------------- #\n\n    @classmethod\n    def auto_discover(cls, module_paths: Optional[list[str]] = None) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Optionally discover handlers by importing modules listed in\n        `module_paths`.  This is useful for Lambda cold-starts where\n        handlers live in separate files and have to be imported (and\n        thus executed) before their decorators register.\n        \"\"\"\n        if not module_paths:\n            return\n        for mod_path in module_paths:\n            try:\n                importlib.import_module(mod_path)\n            except Exception:  # pylint: disable=broad-except\n                log.exception(\"Could not auto-import module %s during discovery\", mod_path)\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n#                               Command Bus                                   #\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n\nclass CommandBus:\n    \"\"\"\n    Thin orchestrator that locates the appropriate handler for an\n    incoming Command instance (via CommandRegistry) and executes it.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        ecs_gateway: ECSGateway,\n        discovery_modules: Optional[list[str]] = None,\n        logger: Optional[logging.Logger] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        self._ecs_gateway = ecs_gateway\n        self._logger = logger or log.getChild(\"bus\")\n        # Ensure registry contains all handlers.\n        CommandRegistry.auto_discover(discovery_modules)\n\n    # --------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n    def dispatch(self, command: Command) -> Any:\n        \"\"\"\n        Execute *command* synchronously.  The function will:\n\n        1. Resolve a registered handler.\n        2. Perform optional validation.\n        3. Run `handler.handle()`.\n        4. Record structured audit in the log.\n\n        Any exception propagates unless it is a known *CommandError*,\n        in which case a clean log statement is emitted first.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        start_ts = time.time_ns()\n\n        handler_cls = CommandRegistry.handler_for(type(command))\n        if handler_cls is None:\n            raise CommandHandlerNotFound(f\"No handler registered for {type(command).__name__}\")\n\n        handler = handler_cls(ecs_gateway=self._ecs_gateway, logger=self._logger)\n\n        try:\n            if hasattr(handler, \"validate\"):\n                handler.validate(command)  # type: ignore[arg-type]\n            result = handler.handle(command)  # type: ignore[arg-type]\n            duration_ms = (time.time_ns() - start_ts) / 1_000_000\n            self._logger.info(\n                \"Command executed: cmd=%s tenant=%s user=%s handler=%s duration=%.2fms\",\n                type(command).__name__,\n                command.tenant_id,\n                command.user_id,\n                handler_cls.__qualname__,\n                duration_ms,\n            )\n            return result\n        except CommandValidationError as exc:\n            self._logger.warning(\n                \"Command validation failed: cmd=%s id=%s reason=%s\",\n                type(command).__name__,\n                command.command_id,\n                exc,\n            )\n            raise\n        except Exception:\n            # Serious errors – log as ERROR and propagate.\n            self._logger.exception(\n                \"Command execution failed: cmd=%s id=%s\", type(command).__name__, command.command_id\n            )\n            raise\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n#                       Sugar – user-friendly decorator                       #\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n\ndef command_handler(\n    command_cls: Type[T_Command],\n) -> \"Callable[[Type[CommandHandler[T_Command]]], Type[CommandHandler[T_Command]]]\":\n    \"\"\"\n    Shorthand decorator, so users can write:\n\n        @command_handler(SpawnNPC)\n        class SpawnNPCHandler(CommandHandler[SpawnNPC]):\n            ...\n\n    instead of using `CommandRegistry.command_handler`.\n    \"\"\"\n    return CommandRegistry.command_handler(command_cls)\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n#                     Example Concrete Command & Handler                      #\n#               (kept here for documentation and unit testing)                #\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n# To avoid circular dependencies in type-checking / import, we\n# explicitly place demo code under a `__name__ == \"__main__\"` guard so\n# it is ignored by production runtime but used by `pytest -m example`.\n\nif __name__.endswith(\".command.__main__\"):\n    # Only executed during `python -m ledgerquest.engine.core.command`.\n    from typing import Mapping\n\n    class InMemoryECS(ECSGateway):\n        \"\"\"Simple in-memory ECS for smoke-tests.\"\"\"\n\n        def __init__(self) -> None:\n            self._store: Dict[str, Dict[str, Mapping[str, Any]]] = {}\n\n        def apply_component_patch(\n            self,\n            entity_id: str,\n            component_name: str,\n            payload: Mapping[str, Any],\n            *,\n            tenant_id: str,\n            user_id: str,\n        ) -> None:\n            entity = self._store.setdefault(entity_id, {})\n            comp = dict(entity.get(component_name, {}))\n            comp.update(payload)\n            entity[component_name] = comp\n            log.debug(\n                \"[TEST] %s/%s – Patched %s: %s\", tenant_id, entity_id, component_name, payload\n            )\n\n        def fetch_component(\n            self, entity_id: str, component_name: str, *, tenant_id: str\n        ) -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n            return self._store.get(entity_id, {}).get(component_name, {})\n\n    # --------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n    #                  Example domain-specific Command                       #\n    # --------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n    @dataclass(frozen=True, kw_only=True)\n    class SetHealth(Command):\n        entity_id: str\n        new_hp: int\n\n    @command_handler(SetHealth)\n    class SetHealthHandler(CommandHandler[SetHealth]):\n        command_cls = SetHealth\n\n        def validate(self, command: SetHealth) -> None:  # \"\"\"\n   235→        with self._lock:\n   236→            if not self._buffer:\n   237→                _LOGGER.debug(\"No audit records to flush.\")\n   238→                return\n   239→\n   240→            records = self._buffer\n   241→            self._buffer = []\n   242→\n   243→        _LOGGER.debug(\"Flushing %d audit records ...\", len(records))\n   244→\n   245→        for chunk in The writer batches audit log records and\npersists them to a DynamoDB table while also emitting them to the Python\nlogging subsystem for CloudWatch log shipping.\n\nDesign goals\n============\n\n1.  **Low-latency hot path** – game logic must not block on I/O.\n2.  **Serverless constraints** – executions may be frozen or force-ended at any\n    time. The simulator receives a\nserialized “world-slice” describing entities that contain physics components,\nadvances the simulation by `delta_time`, and returns the new state.\n\nUnlike traditional long-running simulations, LedgerQuest keeps no in-memory\nstate between invocations—making the update routine *purely functional* and\ntherefore horizontally scalable.  Long-lived state is externalised to\nDynamoDB/S3 by higher-level orchestration layers.\n\nKey Features\n------------\n• 2-D rigid-body integration with semi-implicit Euler\n• Broad-phase AABB collision culling\n• Narrow-phase contact resolution (elastic impulse)\n• Deterministic float math w/ configurable tolerance\n• Structured logging for audit/trace pipelines\n• Graceful error handling with typed exceptions\n\nNotes\n-----\nThis implementation purposely avoids heavyweight, C-accelerated libraries\n(e.g. PyBox2D, Pymunk) so that it can run inside Lambda’s execution limits\nwithout native extensions.  If you deploy this in a container with those\nlibraries available, simply replace the math backend while keeping the\nfunction signatures stable.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport logging\nimport math\nimport os\nimport uuid\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field, asdict\nfrom typing import Dict, Iterable, List, Optional, Tuple\n\n# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Logging Configuration\n# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nLOG_LEVEL = os.getenv(\"LEDGERQUEST_PHYSICS_LOG_LEVEL\", \"INFO\").upper()\nlogging.basicConfig(\n    level=LOG_LEVEL,\n    format=\"%(asctime)s | %(levelname)s | %(name)s | %(message)s\",\n)\nlogger = logging.getLogger(\"ledgerquest.engine.physics.simulator\")\n\n\n# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Exceptions\n# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nclass PhysicsError(RuntimeError):\n    \"\"\"Base-class for physics-simulation errors.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass DeserializationError(PhysicsError):\n    \"\"\"Invalid or malformed input payload raised during deserialization.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass SimulationInvariantViolation(PhysicsError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when the simulation produces a non-finite or invalid result.\"\"\"\n\n\n# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Math Primitives\n# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n@dataclass(slots=True, frozen=True)\nclass Vec2:\n    \"\"\"Immutable 2-D vector with basic operations—fast enough for small N.\"\"\"\n    x: float\n    y: float\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Vector arithmetic\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    def __add__(self, other: \"Vec2\") -> \"Vec2\":\n        return Vec2(self.x + other.x, self.y + other.y)\n\n    def __sub__(self, other: \"Vec2\") -> \"Vec2\":\n        return Vec2(self.x - other.x, self.y - other.y)\n\n    def __mul__(self, scalar: float) -> \"Vec2\":\n        return Vec2(self.x * scalar, self.y * scalar)\n\n    __rmul__ = __mul__\n\n    def dot(self, other: \"Vec2\") -> float:\n        return (self.x * other.x) + (self.y * other.y)\n\n    def magnitude(self) -> float:\n        return math.hypot(self.x, self.y)\n\n    def normalize(self) -> \"Vec2\":\n        mag = self.magnitude() or 1.0\n        return Vec2(self.x / mag, self.y / mag)\n\n    def perpendicular(self) -> \"Vec2\":\n        \"\"\"Right-handed perpendicular vector (rotated 90°).\"\"\"\n        return Vec2(-self.y, self.x)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Built-ins helpers\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    def __iter__(self):\n        yield self.x\n        yield self.y\n\n    def to_json(self) -> Tuple[float, float]:\n        return self.x, self.y\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def from_iterable(it: Iterable[float]) -> \"Vec2\":\n        x, y, *_ = it\n        return Vec2(float(x), float(y))\n\n\n# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Physics Data Structures\n# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n@dataclass(slots=True)\nclass AABB:\n    \"\"\"Axis-Aligned Bounding Box used for broad-phase collision tests.\"\"\"\n    min: Vec2\n    max: Vec2\n\n    def overlaps(self, other: \"AABB\") -> bool:\n        return (\n            self.max.x >= other.min.x\n            and self.min.x <= other.max.x\n            and self.max.y >= other.min.y\n            and self.min.y <= other.max.y\n        )\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_center(cls, center: Vec2, half_extents: Vec2) -> \"AABB\":\n        return cls(\n            min=center - half_extents,\n            max=center + half_extents,\n        )\n\n\n@dataclass(slots=True)\nclass RigidBody:\n    \"\"\"\n    Minimal rigid-body representation.\n\n    Only rectangle shapes are supported in this reference implementation, but\n    the component data model can be extended without changing the simulator’s\n    public interface.\n    \"\"\"\n    id: str\n    position: Vec2\n    velocity: Vec2\n    half_extents: Vec2  # half-width/height (meters)\n    mass: float = 1.0\n    restitution: float = 0.5\n    static: bool = False\n    # Non-serialized runtime fields\n    force_acc: Vec2 = field(default_factory=lambda: Vec2(0.0, 0.0), repr=False)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Convenience wrappers\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    @property\n    def inv_mass(self) -> float:\n        \"\"\"Inverse mass—0 for static or infinite-mass bodies.\"\"\"\n        return 0.0 if self.static else 1.0 / self.mass\n\n    @property\n    def aabb(self) -> AABB:\n        return AABB.from_center(self.position, self.half_extents)\n\n    def apply_force(self, force: Vec2):\n        \"\"\"Accumulate force for the current frame (F = m * a).\"\"\"\n        if not self.static:\n            self.force_acc = self.force_acc + force\n\n\n# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Physics Simulator\n# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nclass PhysicsSimulator:\n    \"\"\"\n    Stateless 2-D physics simulator.\n\n    Usage (inside Lambda):\n    ----------------------\n    def handler(event, context):\n        return PhysicsSimulator.lambda_handler(event, context)\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # Gravity can be overridden at runtime via env-var for gameplay tuning.\n    GRAVITY = Vec2(\n        float(os.getenv(\"LEDGERQUEST_GRAVITY_X\", \"0\")),\n        float(os.getenv(\"LEDGERQUEST_GRAVITY_Y\", \"-9.81\")),\n    )\n\n    # Velocity threshold below which bodies will be considered “sleeping”.\n    SLEEP_TOLERANCE = float(os.getenv(\"LEDGERQUEST_SLEEP_TOLERANCE\", \"0.01\"))\n\n    # Safe-guard to avoid unbounded compute for huge payloads.\n    MAX_BODIES_PER_INVOCATION = int(os.getenv(\"LEDGERQUEST_MAX_BODIES\", \"512\"))\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Lambda Entrypoint\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    @classmethod\n    def lambda_handler(cls, event, _context):\n        \"\"\"\n        AWS Lambda handler. Receives JSON payload:\n        {\n          \"delta_time\": 0.016,\n          \"bodies\": [\n              {\n                  \"id\": \"entity-uuid\",\n                  \"position\": [x, y],\n                  \"velocity\": [vx, vy],\n                  \"half_extents\": [hx, hy],\n                  \"mass\": 1.0,\n                  \"restitution\": 0.5,\n                  \"static\": false\n              },\n              ...\n          ]\n        }\n        \"\"\"\n        try:\n            world_state = cls._deserialize_event(event)\n        except DeserializationError as exc:\n            logger.exception(\"Invalid input payload.\")\n            raise\n\n        logger.debug(\"Simulating %d bodies (dt=%.6f)\",\n                     len(world_state), event[\"delta_time\"])\n\n        cls._validate_payload_size(len(world_state))\n\n        cls._integrate(world_state, event[\"delta_time\"])\n        cls._broad_and_narrow_phase(world_state)\n\n        # Prepare JSON-serialisable response\n        response = {\n            \"bodies\": [\n                {\n                    \"id\": body.id,\n                    \"position\": body.position.to_json(),\n                    \"velocity\": body.velocity.to_json(),\n                }\n                for body in world_state\n            ]\n        }\n        return json.dumps(response)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Public helper for unit tests or in-process calls\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    @classmethod\n    def step(\n        cls,\n        bodies: List[RigidBody],\n        delta_time: float,\n        *,\n        apply_gravity: bool = True,\n    ) -> List[RigidBody]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Advance simulation by `delta_time` and return *mutated* bodies list.\n\n        The method is kept pure: no network, no I/O, no global state mutation,\n        which makes it deterministic and easy to unit-test.\n        \"\"\"\n        if apply_gravity:\n            for body in bodies:\n                if not body.static:\n                    body.apply_force(cls.GRAVITY * body.mass)\n\n        cls._integrate(bodies, delta_time)\n        cls._broad_and_narrow_phase(bodies)\n        return bodies\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internal – Payload (de)serialization\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    @staticmethod\n    def _deserialize_event(event: Dict) -> List[RigidBody]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Convert the inbound JSON event into a list of `RigidBody` instances.\n        Raises `DeserializationError` when invalid.\n        \"\"\"\n        try:\n            bodies_payload = event[\"bodies\"]\n            delta_time = float(event[\"delta_time\"])\n            if delta_time <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(delta_time):\n                raise ValueError(\"delta_time must be positive and finite.\")\n        except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError) as exc:\n            raise DeserializationError(str(exc)) from exc\n\n        bodies = []\n        for raw in bodies_payload:\n            try:\n                body = RigidBody(\n                    id=raw.get(\"id\") or str(uuid.uuid4()),\n                    position=Vec2.from_iterable(raw[\"position\"]),\n                    velocity=Vec2.from_iterable(raw[\"velocity\"]),\n                    half_extents=Vec2.from_iterable(raw[\"half_extents\"]),\n                    mass=float(raw.get(\"mass\", 1.0)),\n                    restitution=float(raw.get(\"restitution\", 0.5)),\n                    static=bool(raw.get(\"static\", False)),\n                )\n                bodies.append(body)\n            except Exception as exc:\n                raise DeserializationError(\n                    f\"Malformed body entry: {raw}\"\n                ) from exc\n\n        return bodies\n\n    @classmethod\n    def _validate_payload_size(cls, n: int):\n        if n > cls.MAX_BODIES_PER_INVOCATION:\n            raise PhysicsError(\n                f\"Too many bodies for a single invocation \"\n                f\"({n} > {cls.MAX_BODIES_PER_INVOCATION}).\"\n            )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internal – Integration\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    @staticmethod\n    def _integrate(bodies: Iterable[RigidBody], dt: float):\n        \"\"\"Semi-implicit Euler integration.\"\"\"\n        for body in bodies:\n            if body.static:\n                continue\n\n            # a = F * inv_mass\n            acceleration = body.force_acc * body.inv_mass\n            body.velocity = body.velocity + acceleration * dt\n            body.position = body.position + body.velocity * dt\n\n            # Clear forces for next frame\n            body.force_acc = Vec2(0.0, 0.0)\n\n            # Sleep optimisation\n            if body.velocity.magnitude() < PhysicsSimulator.SLEEP_TOLERANCE:\n                body.velocity = Vec2(0.0, 0.0)\n\n            # Post-integration invariant check\n            if (\n                not math.isfinite(body.position.x)\n                or not math.isfinite(body.position.y)\n            ):\n                raise SimulationInvariantViolation(\n                    f\"Non-finite position for body {body.id}\"\n                )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internal – Collision Phases\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    @staticmethod\n    def _broad_and_narrow_phase(bodies: List[RigidBody]):\n        \"\"\"\n        Perform broad-phase AABB checks followed by narrow-phase impulse\n        resolution for overlapping pairs.\n        \"\"\"\n        # Simple N^2 broadphase due to low `MAX_BODIES_PER_INVOCATION`.\n        for i, a in enumerate(bodies):\n            if a.static:\n                continue\n            for b in bodies[i + 1 :]:\n                # Skip pairs with two static bodies\n                if a.static and b.static:\n                    continue\n\n                if not a.aabb.overlaps(b.aabb):\n                    continue  # broad-phase cull\n\n                PhysicsSimulator._resolve_collision(a, b)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internal – Narrow-phase Resolution\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    @staticmethod\n    def _resolve_collision(a: RigidBody, b: RigidBody):\n        \"\"\"\n        Very small subset of SAT—for axis-aligned rectangles only.\n        Calculates penetration depth along each axis and resolves along the\n        axis of least penetration.\n        \"\"\"\n        # Relative vector\n        n = b.position - a.position\n\n        # Overlap on x axis\n        x_overlap = (a.half_extents.x + b.half_extents.x) - abs(n.x)\n        if x_overlap <= 0:\n            return  # no collision\n\n        # Overlap on y axis\n        y_overlap = (a.half_extents.y + b.half_extents.y) - abs(n.y)\n        if y_overlap <= 0:\n            return  # no collision\n\n        # Find axis of least penetration\n        if x_overlap < y_overlap:\n            mtv = Vec2(x_overlap if n.x > 0 else -x_overlap, 0)\n        else:\n            mtv = Vec2(0, y_overlap if n.y > 0 else -y_overlap)\n\n        # Positional correction (minimum translation vector)\n        PhysicsSimulator._positional_correction(a, b, mtv)\n\n        # Relative velocity\n        rv = b.velocity - a.velocity\n        # Normal is direction of MTV\n        normal = mtv.normalize()\n\n        vel_along_normal = rv.dot(normal)\n        if vel_along_normal > 0:\n            return  # bodies separating\n\n        restitution = min(a.restitution, b.restitution)\n\n        # Impulse scalar\n        j = -(1 + restitution) * vel_along_normal\n        inv_mass_sum = a.inv_mass + b.inv_mass\n        if inv_mass_sum == 0:\n            return\n\n        j /= inv_mass_sum\n\n        # Apply impulse\n        impulse = normal * j\n        if not a.static:\n            a.velocity = a.velocity - impulse * a.inv_mass\n        if not b.static:\n            b.velocity = b.velocity + impulse * b.inv_mass\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internal – Positional Correction\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    @staticmethod\n    def _positional_correction(a: RigidBody, b: RigidBody, mtv: Vec2):\n        \"\"\"\n        Positional correction to avoid sinking due to numerical errors.\n        \"\"\"\n        percent = 0.8  # higher percentage = more correction\n        slop = 0.01    # penetration allowance\n        correction_magnitude = max(mtv.magnitude() - slop, 0.0) * percent\n        correction = mtv.normalize() * correction_magnitude\n\n        total_inv_mass = a.inv_mass + b.inv_mass\n        if total_inv_mass == 0:\n            return\n\n        if not a.static:\n            a.position = a.position - correction * (a.inv_mass / total_inv_mass)\n        if not b.static:\n            b.position = b.position + correction * (b.inv_mass / total_inv_mass)\n\n\n# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Module test hook (executed only when run locally)\n# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    # Quick sanity test – two boxes falling onto the floor.\n    ground = RigidBody(\n        id=\"ground\",\n        position=Vec2(0.0, -1.0),\n        velocity=Vec2(0.0, 0.0),\n        half_extents=Vec2(5.0, 0.5),\n        static=True,\n    )\n\n    box = RigidBody(\n        id=\"box\",\n        position=Vec2(0.0, 2.0),\n        velocity=Vec2(0.0, 0.0),\n        half_extents=Vec2(0.5, 0.5),\n        mass=1.0,\n        restitution=0.3,\n    )\n\n    bodies = [ground, box]\n    dt = 1.0 / 60.0\n\n    for frame in range(240):  # 4 seconds\n        PhysicsSimulator.step(bodies, dt)\n        logger.info(\n            \"Frame %03d | pos=%.3f, %.3f vel=%.3f, %.3f\",\n            frame,\n            box.position.x,\n            box.position.y,\n            box.velocity.x,\n            box.velocity.y,\n        )\n```","numLines":505,"startLine":1,"totalLines":505}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_018PHsfkEUpPAJvjbHTZLVcf","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01WiqEgK8J9pxZMUzkvMbU62","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/logs/agent/solution.md","content":"# LedgerQuest Engine — Game Replay System Technical Design\n\n| Attribute       | Value                              |\n|-----------------|------------------------------------|\n| **Status**      | Proposed                           |\n| **Date**        | 2026-02-02                         |\n| **Author**      | Architecture Team                  |\n| **Tags**        | replay, game-loop, serverless, s3  |\n\n---\n\n## 1. Overview\n\nThis document proposes a technical design for a **Game Replay System** for the LedgerQuest Engine. The system will enable players and administrators to watch recordings of past game sessions, supporting use cases including debugging, content creation, and potential e-sports integration.\n\n### Proposed Solution Summary\n\nThe replay system will capture **validated player inputs (commands)** after the `InputProcessor` step of the game loop, storing them in **Amazon S3** organized by game session and tick. For playback, a dedicated **Replay Service** will stream this input data to clients, which will use a local instance of the deterministic game engine to reconstruct the session tick-by-tick. This approach leverages the engine's deterministic nature to minimize storage costs while ensuring accurate replay fidelity.\n\n**Key Design Decisions:**\n1. **Input Capture Strategy** — Capture validated commands rather than full state snapshots\n2. **S3-based Storage** — Cost-effective, scalable storage for replay data\n3. **Client-side Reconstruction** — Leverage determinism for accurate, bandwidth-efficient playback\n4. **Non-blocking Capture** — Asynchronous writes to minimize impact on live game loop\n\n---\n\n## 2. Data Capture Strategy\n\n### 2.1 Analysis of Capture Points\n\nThe LedgerQuest game loop, orchestrated by the AWS Step Function defined in `infra/step_functions/game_loop_statemachine.asl.json`, follows this sequence:\n\n```\nInitializeFrame → ProcessInput → UpdateAI → PhysicsStep → RenderFrame → PersistState → WaitForNextTick → LoopDecider\n```\n\nTwo primary capture points were identified:\n\n| Capture Point | Location | Data Type | Files Involved |\n|--------------|----------|-----------|----------------|\n| **Input Capture** | After `ProcessInput` step | Validated commands | `ledgerquest/services/game_loop/input_processor.py:279-288` |\n| **State Capture** | During/after `PersistState` step | Full game state or deltas | `ledgerquest/services/game_loop/state_committer.py:182-265` |\n\n### 2.2 Recommended Approach: Input Capture\n\n**We recommend capturing data after the `InputProcessor` step** (`ledgerquest/services/game_loop/input_processor.py`).\n\nAt this point in the game loop, raw player interactions have been:\n1. Validated via JSON schema (`input_processor.py:123-143`)\n2. Checked for idempotency (`input_processor.py:250-273`)\n3. Converted to canonical `Command` objects (`input_processor.py:279-288`)\n\nThe `Command` dataclass (defined at `input_processor.py:87-101`) contains:\n\n```python\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Command:\n    tenant_id: str\n    game_id: str\n    player_id: str\n    command: InputType  # MOVE, CAST_SKILL, CHAT, PAUSE\n    payload: Dict[str, Any]\n    interaction_id: str\n```\n\n### 2.3 Justification: Why Input Capture Over State Capture\n\n| Criterion | Input Capture | State Capture |\n|-----------|---------------|---------------|\n| **Storage Size** | ~100 bytes/command | ~10-100 KB/tick |\n| **Storage Cost (1hr session @ 30 tps)** | ~10 MB | ~1-10 GB |\n| **Replay Accuracy** | Exact (deterministic) | Exact |\n| **Implementation Complexity** | Lower | Higher |\n| **Dependency on Determinism** | **Yes (critical)** | No |\n| **Engine Version Sensitivity** | High | Low |\n\n**Key Trade-offs:**\n\n1. **Storage Efficiency**: Input capture is orders of magnitude more efficient. A 1-hour session at 30 ticks/second with ~10 commands per tick would produce:\n   - Input: `30 × 3600 × 10 × 100 bytes ≈ 108 MB`\n   - State: `30 × 3600 × 50 KB ≈ 5.4 GB`\n\n2. **Determinism Requirement**: Input capture *requires* the game engine to be fully deterministic. Based on analysis of the codebase:\n   - The physics simulator (`ledgerquest/engine/physics/simulator.py`) uses deterministic float math with configurable tolerance (`simulator.py:64-67`)\n   - ADR-001 explicitly mentions \"deterministic state\" as a requirement (`docs/architecture/adr/001-serverless-game-loop.md:27-28`)\n   - The data model documentation mentions \"EventStore acts as the single source of truth to rebuild every game state exactly as it unfolded\" (`docs/architecture/data_model.md:24-25`)\n\n3. **Fallback Strategy**: We recommend implementing a **hybrid approach** as a fallback:\n   - Capture inputs every tick (primary)\n   - Capture periodic state snapshots (e.g., every 60 seconds) as checkpoints\n   - This enables \"fast-forward\" seeking and recovery from any non-determinism bugs\n\n### 2.4 Capture Implementation Point\n\nThe optimal integration point is within the `_publish_command` method of `InputProcessor` (`input_processor.py:294-323`). After successful EventBridge publication, emit the command to the replay capture system:\n\n```\nLocation: ledgerquest/services/game_loop/input_processor.py:294-323\nMethod: _publish_command()\nTrigger: After successful eb.put_events() call\n```\n\nAlternatively, add a new Step Function state after `ProcessInput` that writes to S3 in parallel with the main game loop continuation.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Data Storage and Management\n\n### 3.1 Recommended Storage Service: Amazon S3\n\n**Primary Service**: Amazon S3\n**Rationale**:\n\n| Requirement | S3 Capability |\n|-------------|---------------|\n| Cost | ~$0.023/GB/month (Standard), ~$0.004/GB (Glacier) |\n| Scalability | Virtually unlimited |\n| Durability | 99.999999999% (11 9's) |\n| Retrieval Pattern | Sequential reads (ideal for replays) |\n| Integration | Native Lambda/Step Functions support |\n\nS3 is the logical choice because:\n1. **Low cost** — Replay data is write-once, read-occasionally\n2. **Lifecycle policies** — Automatic transition to Glacier after configurable period\n3. **Existing patterns** — The codebase already uses S3 for state overflow (`state_committer.py:299-343`) and asset storage\n\n### 3.2 Data Format and Schema\n\n#### S3 Object Structure\n\n```\ns3://ledgerquest-replays-{env}-{region}/\n├── {tenant_id}/\n│   └── {game_id}/\n│       ├── manifest.json\n│       ├── initial_state.json.gz\n│       ├── ticks/\n│       │   ├── 000000-000999.jsonl.gz\n│       │   ├── 001000-001999.jsonl.gz\n│       │   └── ...\n│       └── checkpoints/\n│           ├── checkpoint-0060.json.gz\n│           ├── checkpoint-0120.json.gz\n│           └── ...\n```\n\n#### Manifest Schema (`manifest.json`)\n\n```json\n{\n  \"version\": \"1.0\",\n  \"game_id\": \"abc123\",\n  \"tenant_id\": \"corp-foo\",\n  \"engine_version\": \"2.3.1\",\n  \"physics_seed\": 91356012,\n  \"config_hash\": \"sha256:84dd8c3d3...\",\n  \"started_at\": \"2026-01-15T10:30:00Z\",\n  \"ended_at\": \"2026-01-15T11:45:00Z\",\n  \"total_ticks\": 162000,\n  \"tick_rate_hz\": 30,\n  \"players\": [\n    {\"player_id\": \"p1\", \"joined_tick\": 0},\n    {\"player_id\": \"p2\", \"joined_tick\": 150}\n  ],\n  \"checkpoints\": [\n    {\"tick\": 1800, \"s3_key\": \"checkpoints/checkpoint-0060.json.gz\"},\n    {\"tick\": 3600, \"s3_key\": \"checkpoints/checkpoint-0120.json.gz\"}\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n#### Tick Data Format (JSONL, gzipped)\n\nEach line in the tick file represents one tick's worth of commands:\n\n```json\n{\"tick\": 0, \"ts\": 1705315800000, \"cmds\": []}\n{\"tick\": 1, \"ts\": 1705315800033, \"cmds\": [{\"p\": \"p1\", \"t\": \"MOVE\", \"d\": {\"x\": 1, \"y\": 0}}]}\n{\"tick\": 2, \"ts\": 1705315800066, \"cmds\": [{\"p\": \"p1\", \"t\": \"CAST_SKILL\", \"d\": {\"skill\": \"fireball\", \"target\": [5, 3]}}]}\n```\n\nThe compact format uses:\n- `p` = player_id\n- `t` = command type (InputType enum value)\n- `d` = payload data\n- `ts` = timestamp (epoch milliseconds)\n\n### 3.3 Data Lifecycle\n\n| Stage | Age | Storage Class | Access |\n|-------|-----|---------------|--------|\n| Active | 0-7 days | S3 Standard | Frequent |\n| Archive | 7-90 days | S3 Standard-IA | Occasional |\n| Cold | 90+ days | S3 Glacier | Rare |\n| Delete | 365+ days | N/A | Configurable per tenant |\n\n### 3.4 Indexing (DynamoDB)\n\nTo support replay discovery, maintain a DynamoDB index table:\n\n```\nTable: ledgerquest-replay-index\n\nPK: TENANT#{tenant_id}\nSK: GAME#{game_id}\n\nAttributes:\n- game_id\n- tenant_id\n- started_at\n- ended_at\n- duration_seconds\n- player_count\n- s3_manifest_key\n- status (RECORDING, COMPLETE, ARCHIVED, DELETED)\n\nGSI1: player_id + started_at (find replays by player)\nGSI2: started_at + tenant_id (chronological listing)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. Replay Playback Mechanism\n\n### 4.1 High-Level Architecture\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                              Replay Service                                  │\n│                                                                              │\n│   ┌───────────────┐    ┌───────────────┐    ┌───────────────┐              │\n│   │  API Gateway  │───►│ Lambda:       │───►│    S3:        │              │\n│   │  (REST)       │    │ ReplayFetcher │    │ Replay Data   │              │\n│   └───────────────┘    └───────────────┘    └───────────────┘              │\n│          │                    │                                             │\n│          │                    ▼                                             │\n│          │             ┌───────────────┐                                   │\n│          │             │  CloudFront   │                                   │\n│          │             │  (Optional)   │                                   │\n│          │             └───────────────┘                                   │\n│          │                    │                                             │\n│          ▼                    ▼                                             │\n│   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐              │\n│   │                     Game Client                          │              │\n│   │  ┌─────────────┐   ┌─────────────┐   ┌─────────────┐    │              │\n│   │  │  Replay     │──►│  Local      │──►│  Renderer   │    │              │\n│   │  │  Controller │   │  Engine     │   │             │    │              │\n│   │  └─────────────┘   └─────────────┘   └─────────────┘    │              │\n│   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘              │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n### 4.2 API Endpoints\n\nThe Replay Service exposes the following REST API endpoints via API Gateway:\n\n#### GET /replays\n**List available replays for a tenant**\n\n```\nGET /replays?tenant_id={tenant_id}&player_id={optional}&limit=20&cursor={optional}\n\nResponse:\n{\n  \"replays\": [\n    {\n      \"game_id\": \"abc123\",\n      \"started_at\": \"2026-01-15T10:30:00Z\",\n      \"duration_seconds\": 4500,\n      \"player_count\": 4,\n      \"status\": \"COMPLETE\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"next_cursor\": \"...\"\n}\n```\n\n#### GET /replays/{game_id}\n**Get replay metadata and manifest**\n\n```\nGET /replays/{game_id}?tenant_id={tenant_id}\n\nResponse:\n{\n  \"manifest\": { ... },  // Full manifest.json content\n  \"download_urls\": {\n    \"initial_state\": \"https://s3.presigned.url/...\",\n    \"tick_chunks\": [\n      {\"range\": \"0-999\", \"url\": \"https://...\"},\n      {\"range\": \"1000-1999\", \"url\": \"https://...\"}\n    ],\n    \"checkpoints\": [...]\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### GET /replays/{game_id}/ticks\n**Stream tick data for a specific range**\n\n```\nGET /replays/{game_id}/ticks?start=0&end=1000\n\nResponse: (streaming JSONL)\n{\"tick\": 0, \"ts\": ..., \"cmds\": [...]}\n{\"tick\": 1, \"ts\": ..., \"cmds\": [...]}\n...\n```\n\n#### GET /replays/{game_id}/checkpoint/{tick}\n**Get nearest checkpoint for seeking**\n\n```\nGET /replays/{game_id}/checkpoint/1800\n\nResponse:\n{\n  \"checkpoint_tick\": 1800,\n  \"state_url\": \"https://s3.presigned.url/...\",\n  \"next_ticks_url\": \"https://s3.presigned.url/...\"\n}\n```\n\n### 4.3 Client-Side Reconstruction\n\nThe client performs local reconstruction using these steps:\n\n1. **Initialize**: Fetch manifest and initial state\n2. **Load Engine**: Initialize local game engine with `engine_version` and `physics_seed` from manifest\n3. **Apply State**: Load `initial_state.json.gz` into engine\n4. **Stream Ticks**: Fetch tick chunks and apply commands sequentially\n5. **Render**: Present each tick's resulting state to the player\n\n**Playback Algorithm (Pseudocode):**\n\n```python\nclass ReplayController:\n    def __init__(self, manifest, engine):\n        self.manifest = manifest\n        self.engine = engine\n        self.current_tick = 0\n        self.tick_buffer = []\n        self.playback_speed = 1.0\n\n    async def play(self):\n        # Load initial state\n        initial_state = await self.fetch_initial_state()\n        self.engine.load_state(initial_state)\n\n        # Start streaming ticks\n        tick_stream = self.stream_ticks(start=0)\n\n        while self.current_tick < self.manifest.total_ticks:\n            tick_data = await tick_stream.next()\n\n            # Apply all commands for this tick\n            for cmd in tick_data.cmds:\n                self.engine.apply_command(cmd)\n\n            # Advance engine simulation\n            self.engine.step()\n\n            # Render frame\n            self.renderer.present(self.engine.get_state())\n\n            # Control playback speed\n            await sleep(1.0 / (self.manifest.tick_rate_hz * self.playback_speed))\n\n            self.current_tick += 1\n\n    async def seek(self, target_tick):\n        # Find nearest checkpoint before target\n        checkpoint = self.find_nearest_checkpoint(target_tick)\n\n        if checkpoint:\n            state = await self.fetch_checkpoint(checkpoint.tick)\n            self.engine.load_state(state)\n            self.current_tick = checkpoint.tick\n        else:\n            # Replay from beginning\n            initial_state = await self.fetch_initial_state()\n            self.engine.load_state(initial_state)\n            self.current_tick = 0\n\n        # Fast-forward to target tick (no rendering)\n        while self.current_tick < target_tick:\n            tick_data = await self.get_tick(self.current_tick)\n            for cmd in tick_data.cmds:\n                self.engine.apply_command(cmd)\n            self.engine.step()\n            self.current_tick += 1\n```\n\n### 4.4 Integration with Existing Engine\n\nThe replay system reuses existing engine components:\n\n| Component | File | Usage in Replay |\n|-----------|------|-----------------|\n| Command processing | `ledgerquest/engine/core/command.py` | Apply recorded commands |\n| Physics simulation | `ledgerquest/engine/physics/simulator.py` | Deterministic tick advancement |\n| AI behavior | `ledgerquest/services/game_loop/ai_updater.py` | Re-execute AI decisions |\n| State serialization | `ledgerquest/engine/state/serializer.py` | Load/save checkpoints |\n| ECS system | `ledgerquest/engine/ecs/*.py` | Entity/component management |\n\nThe key insight is that the existing `PhysicsSimulator.step()` method (`simulator.py:267-288`) is explicitly designed to be **pure and deterministic**:\n\n```python\n# From ledgerquest/engine/physics/simulator.py:267-288\n@classmethod\ndef step(cls, bodies: List[RigidBody], delta_time: float, *, apply_gravity: bool = True) -> List[RigidBody]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Advance simulation by `delta_time` and return *mutated* bodies list.\n\n    The method is kept pure: no network, no I/O, no global state mutation,\n    which makes it deterministic and easy to unit-test.\n    \"\"\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. Architectural Impact and Risks\n\n### 5.1 Performance Impact on Live Game Loop\n\n#### Latency Analysis\n\n| Operation | Current Latency | Added Latency | Mitigation |\n|-----------|-----------------|---------------|------------|\n| `ProcessInput` | ~50ms | +5-15ms | Async S3 write |\n| `PersistState` | ~15-45ms | +0ms | No change |\n| Total tick | ~100ms | +5-15ms | Non-blocking pattern |\n\n**Mitigation Strategy: Non-Blocking Writes**\n\nInstead of synchronous S3 writes in the critical path, we propose:\n\n1. **Option A: Async Lambda Invocation**\n   - After `ProcessInput`, invoke a separate \"ReplayCapture\" Lambda asynchronously\n   - Zero added latency to game loop\n   - Eventual consistency (commands may be lost if Lambda fails)\n\n2. **Option B: Step Function Parallel State** (Recommended)\n   - Modify the state machine to add a parallel branch after `ProcessInput`\n   - One branch continues the game loop\n   - Other branch writes to S3\n   - Guaranteed delivery without blocking\n\n```json\n{\n  \"Type\": \"Parallel\",\n  \"Branches\": [\n    {\n      \"StartAt\": \"CaptureReplayData\",\n      \"States\": {\n        \"CaptureReplayData\": {\n          \"Type\": \"Task\",\n          \"Resource\": \"arn:aws:states:::lambda:invoke\",\n          \"Parameters\": {\n            \"FunctionName\": \"${ReplayCaptureFunction}\",\n            \"Payload.$\": \"$\"\n          },\n          \"End\": true\n        }\n      }\n    },\n    {\n      \"StartAt\": \"UpdateAI\",\n      \"States\": { /* existing game loop states */ }\n    }\n  ],\n  \"Next\": \"WaitForNextTick\"\n}\n```\n\n#### Memory Impact\n\n| Metric | Impact | Notes |\n|--------|--------|-------|\n| Lambda memory | +5-10 MB | Buffering 1 second of commands |\n| DynamoDB capacity | +1-2 WCU/game | Index writes only |\n\n### 5.2 Cost Analysis\n\n#### Storage Costs (Monthly, per 1000 active games)\n\n| Component | Calculation | Monthly Cost |\n|-----------|-------------|--------------|\n| S3 Standard (7 days) | 1000 games × 50MB × 7/30 | ~$0.03 |\n| S3 Standard-IA (83 days) | 1000 games × 50MB × 83/30 | ~$0.35 |\n| S3 Glacier (275 days) | 1000 games × 50MB × 275/30 | ~$0.18 |\n| DynamoDB Index | 1000 games × 1KB × 0.25 | ~$0.25 |\n| **Total Storage** | | **~$0.81/month** |\n\n#### Compute Costs (per 1000 game sessions)\n\n| Component | Calculation | Cost |\n|-----------|-------------|------|\n| Capture Lambda | 1000 games × 108K ticks × 128MB × 50ms | ~$0.90 |\n| Replay Lambda | Assuming 10% replay rate, 1000 requests | ~$0.02 |\n| API Gateway | 100 replay sessions × 1000 requests | ~$0.35 |\n| **Total Compute** | | **~$1.27/1000 sessions** |\n\n**Total incremental cost: ~$0.002 per game session**\n\n### 5.3 Risks and Mitigations\n\n#### Risk 1: Engine Determinism Violations (CRITICAL)\n\n**Risk**: If the game engine is not perfectly deterministic, replays will diverge from the original session.\n\n**Probability**: Medium — The codebase shows determinism intent but doesn't enforce it everywhere.\n\n**Impact**: High — Corrupted replays damage player trust.\n\n**Detection Indicators**:\n- `random.random()` calls without seeding (found in `ai_updater.py:127-129`)\n- Floating-point operations across different architectures\n- Hash iteration order in Python < 3.7\n- Time-dependent logic\n\n**Mitigations**:\n1. Introduce periodic checkpoints (every 60 seconds) to bound divergence\n2. Add determinism validation tests to CI/CD pipeline\n3. Log hash of game state every N ticks for divergence detection\n4. Version lock engine binaries in replay manifest\n\n#### Risk 2: Engine Version Compatibility (HIGH)\n\n**Risk**: Game engine updates may break playback of old replays.\n\n**Probability**: High — Game engines evolve continuously.\n\n**Impact**: High — Historical replays become unplayable.\n\n**Mitigations**:\n1. **Version tagging**: Store `engine_version` in manifest (`manifest.json`)\n2. **Engine versioning**: Package engine as Lambda Layers with version tags\n3. **Compatibility matrix**: Maintain documentation of breaking changes\n4. **Replay migration**: Build tooling to re-record old replays with new engine\n\n```python\n# Proposed engine version check in replay controller\nclass ReplayController:\n    def validate_compatibility(self, manifest):\n        current_version = self.engine.get_version()  # e.g., \"2.3.1\"\n        replay_version = manifest.engine_version      # e.g., \"2.2.0\"\n\n        if not is_compatible(current_version, replay_version):\n            raise IncompatibleReplayError(\n                f\"Replay requires engine {replay_version}, \"\n                f\"current engine is {current_version}\"\n            )\n```\n\n#### Risk 3: Storage Cost Explosion\n\n**Risk**: Long games or high-frequency events could generate excessive data.\n\n**Probability**: Low — Our estimates show ~50MB per hour.\n\n**Impact**: Medium — Cost overruns.\n\n**Mitigations**:\n1. Implement per-game-session size limits (e.g., 500MB)\n2. Add tenant-level quotas for total replay storage\n3. Aggressive lifecycle policies (Glacier after 30 days)\n4. Consider delta compression for command sequences\n\n#### Risk 4: Replay Data Loss\n\n**Risk**: Async writes may fail silently, resulting in incomplete replays.\n\n**Probability**: Low — S3 is highly durable.\n\n**Impact**: Medium — Incomplete replays.\n\n**Mitigations**:\n1. Use Step Function parallel branch (guaranteed execution)\n2. Implement end-of-game validation that checks tick count\n3. Mark replays as \"INCOMPLETE\" if validation fails\n4. DLQ for failed capture operations with retry logic\n\n### 5.4 Security Considerations\n\n| Concern | Mitigation |\n|---------|------------|\n| Multi-tenant isolation | S3 bucket policies enforce `tenant_id` prefix |\n| Data access | Pre-signed URLs with short TTL (15 minutes) |\n| Sensitive data in commands | Filter/redact chat messages before storage |\n| Replay manipulation | Include HMAC signature in manifest |\n| Rate limiting | API Gateway throttling (100 req/sec/tenant) |\n\n---\n\n## 6. Overview\n\nThis document proposes a technical design for a **Game Replay System** for the LedgerQuest Engine. The system will enable players and administrators to watch recordings of past game sessions, supporting use cases including debugging, content creation, and potential e-sports integration.\n\n### Proposed Solution Summary\n\nThe replay system will capture **validated player inputs (commands)** after the `InputProcessor` step of the game loop, storing them in **Amazon S3** organized by game session and tick. For playback, a dedicated **Replay Service** will stream this input data to clients, which will use a local instance of the deterministic game engine to reconstruct the session tick-by-tick. This approach leverages the engine's deterministic nature to minimize storage costs while ensuring accurate replay fidelity.\n\n**Key Design Decisions:**\n1. **Input Capture Strategy** — Capture validated commands rather than full state snapshots\n2. **S3-based Storage** — Cost-effective, scalable storage for replay data\n3. **Client-side Reconstruction** — Leverage determinism for accurate, bandwidth-efficient playback\n4. **Non-blocking Capture** — Asynchronous writes to minimize impact on live game loop\n\n---\n\n## 2. Data Capture Strategy\n\n### 2.1 Analysis of Capture Points\n\nThe LedgerQuest game loop, orchestrated by the AWS Step Function defined in `infra/step_functions/game_loop_statemachine.asl.json`, follows this sequence:\n\n```\nInitializeFrame → ProcessInput → UpdateAI → PhysicsStep → RenderFrame → PersistState → WaitForNextTick → LoopDecider\n```\n\nTwo primary capture points were identified:\n\n| Capture Point | Location | Data Type | Files Involved |\n|--------------|----------|-----------|----------------|\n| **Input Capture** | After `ProcessInput` step | Validated commands | `ledgerquest/services/game_loop/input_processor.py:279-288` |\n| **State Capture** | During/after `PersistState` step | Full game state or deltas | `ledgerquest/services/game_loop/state_committer.py:182-265` |\n\n### 2.2 Recommended Approach: Input Capture\n\n**We recommend capturing data after the `InputProcessor` step** (`ledgerquest/services/game_loop/input_processor.py`).\n\nAt this point in the game loop, raw player interactions have been:\n1. Validated via JSON schema (`input_processor.py:123-143`)\n2. Checked for idempotency (`input_processor.py:250-273`)\n3. Converted to canonical `Command` objects (`input_processor.py:279-288`)\n\nThe `Command` dataclass (defined at `input_processor.py:87-101`) contains:\n\n```python\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Command:\n    tenant_id: str\n    game_id: str\n    player_id: str\n    command: InputType  # MOVE, CAST_SKILL, CHAT, PAUSE\n    payload: Dict[str, Any]\n    interaction_id: str\n```\n\n### 2.3 Justification: Why Input Capture Over State Capture\n\n| Criterion | Input Capture | State Capture |\n|-----------|---------------|---------------|\n| **Storage Size** | ~100 bytes/command | ~10-100 KB/tick |\n| **Storage Cost (1hr session @ 30 tps)** | ~10 MB | ~1-10 GB |\n| **Replay Accuracy** | Exact (deterministic) | Exact |\n| **Implementation Complexity** | Lower | Higher |\n| **Dependency on Determinism** | **Yes (critical)** | No |\n| **Engine Version Sensitivity** | High | Low |\n\n**Key Trade-offs:**\n\n1. **Storage Efficiency**: Input capture is orders of magnitude more efficient. A 1-hour session at 30 ticks/second with ~10 commands per tick would produce:\n   - Input: `30 × 3600 × 10 × 100 bytes ≈ 108 MB`\n   - State: `30 × 3600 × 50 KB ≈ 5.4 GB`\n\n2. **Determinism Requirement**: Input capture *requires* the game engine to be fully deterministic. Based on analysis of the codebase:\n   - The physics simulator (`ledgerquest/engine/physics/simulator.py`) uses deterministic float math with configurable tolerance (`simulator.py:64-67`)\n   - ADR-001 explicitly mentions \"deterministic state\" as a requirement (`docs/architecture/adr/001-serverless-game-loop.md:27-28`)\n   - The data model documentation mentions \"EventStore acts as the single source of truth to rebuild every game state exactly as it unfolded\" (`docs/architecture/data_model.md:24-25`)\n\n3. **Fallback Strategy**: We recommend implementing a **hybrid approach** as a fallback:\n   - Capture inputs every tick (primary)\n   - Capture periodic state snapshots (e.g., every 60 seconds) as checkpoints\n   - This enables \"fast-forward\" seeking and recovery from any non-determinism bugs\n\n### 2.4 Capture Implementation Point\n\nThe optimal integration point is within the `_publish_command` method of `InputProcessor` (`input_processor.py:294-323`). After successful EventBridge publication, emit the command to the replay capture system:\n\n```\nLocation: ledgerquest/services/game_loop/input_processor.py:294-323\nMethod: _publish_command()\nTrigger: After successful eb.put_events() call\n```\n\nAlternatively, add a new Step Function state after `ProcessInput` that writes to S3 in parallel with the main game loop continuation.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Data Storage and Management\n\n### 3.1 Recommended Storage Service: Amazon S3\n\n**Primary Service**: Amazon S3\n**Rationale**:\n\n| Requirement | S3 Capability |\n|-------------|---------------|\n| Cost | ~$0.023/GB/month (Standard), ~$0.004/GB (Glacier) |\n| Scalability | Virtually unlimited |\n| Durability | 99.999999999% (11 9's) |\n| Retrieval Pattern | Sequential reads (ideal for replays) |\n| Integration | Native Lambda/Step Functions support |\n\nS3 is the logical choice because:\n1. **Low cost** — Replay data is write-once, read-occasionally\n2. **Lifecycle policies** — Automatic transition to Glacier after configurable period\n3. **Existing patterns** — The codebase already uses S3 for state overflow (`state_committer.py:299-343`) and asset storage\n\n### 3.2 Data Format and Schema\n\n#### S3 Object Structure\n\n```\ns3://ledgerquest-replays-{env}-{region}/\n├── {tenant_id}/\n│   └── {game_id}/\n│       ├── manifest.json\n│       ├── initial_state.json.gz\n│       ├── ticks/\n│       │   ├── 000000-000999.jsonl.gz\n│       │   ├── 001000-001999.jsonl.gz\n│       │   └── ...\n│       └── checkpoints/\n│           ├── checkpoint-0060.json.gz\n│           ├── checkpoint-0120.json.gz\n│           └── ...\n```\n\n#### Manifest Schema (`manifest.json`)\n\n```json\n{\n  \"version\": \"1.0\",\n  \"game_id\": \"abc123\",\n  \"tenant_id\": \"corp-foo\",\n  \"engine_version\": \"2.3.1\",\n  \"physics_seed\": 91356012,\n  \"config_hash\": \"sha256:84dd8c3d3...\",\n  \"started_at\": \"2026-01-15T10:30:00Z\",\n  \"ended_at\": \"2026-01-15T11:45:00Z\",\n  \"total_ticks\": 162000,\n  \"tick_rate_hz\": 30,\n  \"players\": [\n    {\"player_id\": \"p1\", \"joined_tick\": 0},\n    {\"player_id\": \"p2\", \"joined_tick\": 150}\n  ],\n  \"checkpoints\": [\n    {\"tick\": 1800, \"s3_key\": \"checkpoints/checkpoint-0060.json.gz\"},\n    {\"tick\": 3600, \"s3_key\": \"checkpoints/checkpoint-0120.json.gz\"}\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n#### Tick Data Format (JSONL, gzipped)\n\nEach line in the tick file represents one tick's worth of commands:\n\n```json\n{\"tick\": 0, \"ts\": 1705315800000, \"cmds\": []}\n{\"tick\": 1, \"ts\": 1705315800033, \"cmds\": [{\"p\": \"p1\", \"t\": \"MOVE\", \"d\": {\"x\": 1, \"y\": 0}}]}\n{\"tick\": 2, \"ts\": 1705315800066, \"cmds\": [{\"p\": \"p1\", \"t\": \"CAST_SKILL\", \"d\": {\"skill\": \"fireball\", \"target\": [5, 3]}}]}\n```\n\nThe compact format uses:\n- `p` = player_id\n- `t` = command type (InputType enum value)\n- `d` = payload data\n- `ts` = timestamp (epoch milliseconds)\n\n### 3.3 Data Lifecycle\n\n| Stage | Age | Storage Class | Access |\n|-------|-----|---------------|--------|\n| Active | 0-7 days | S3 Standard | Frequent |\n| Archive | 7-90 days | S3 Standard-IA | Occasional |\n| Cold | 90+ days | S3 Glacier | Rare |\n| Delete | 365+ days | N/A | Configurable per tenant |\n\n### 3.4 Indexing (DynamoDB)\n\nTo support replay discovery, maintain a DynamoDB index table:\n\n```\nTable: ledgerquest-replay-index\n\nPK: TENANT#{tenant_id}\nSK: GAME#{game_id}\n\nAttributes:\n- game_id\n- tenant_id\n- started_at\n- ended_at\n- duration_seconds\n- player_count\n- s3_manifest_key\n- status (RECORDING, COMPLETE, ARCHIVED, DELETED)\n\nGSI1: player_id + started_at (find replays by player)\nGSI2: started_at + tenant_id (chronological listing)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. Replay Playback Mechanism\n\n### 4.1 High-Level Architecture\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                              Replay Service                                  │\n│                                                                              │\n│   ┌───────────────┐    ┌───────────────┐    ┌───────────────┐              │\n│   │  API Gateway  │───►│ Lambda:       │───►│    S3:        │              │\n│   │  (REST)       │    │ ReplayFetcher │    │ Replay Data   │              │\n│   └───────────────┘    └───────────────┘    └───────────────┘              │\n│          │                    │                                             │\n│          │                    ▼                                             │\n│          │             ┌───────────────┐                                   │\n│          │             │  CloudFront   │                                   │\n│          │             │  (Optional)   │                                   │\n│          │             └───────────────┘                                   │\n│          │                    │                                             │\n│          ▼                    ▼                                             │\n│   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐              │\n│   │                     Game Client                          │              │\n│   │  ┌─────────────┐   ┌─────────────┐   ┌─────────────┐    │              │\n│   │  │  Replay     │──►│  Local      │──►│  Renderer   │    │              │\n│   │  │  Controller │   │  Engine     │   │             │    │              │\n│   │  └─────────────┘   └─────────────┘   └─────────────┘    │              │\n│   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘              │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n### 4.2 API Endpoints\n\nThe Replay Service exposes the following REST API endpoints via API Gateway:\n\n#### GET /replays\n**List available replays for a tenant**\n\n```\nGET /replays?tenant_id={tenant_id}&player_id={optional}&limit=20&cursor={optional}\n\nResponse:\n{\n  \"replays\": [\n    {\n      \"game_id\": \"abc123\",\n      \"started_at\": \"2026-01-15T10:30:00Z\",\n      \"duration_seconds\": 4500,\n      \"player_count\": 4,\n      \"status\": \"COMPLETE\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"next_cursor\": \"...\"\n}\n```\n\n#### GET /replays/{game_id}\n**Get replay metadata and manifest**\n\n```\nGET /replays/{game_id}?tenant_id={tenant_id}\n\nResponse:\n{\n  \"manifest\": { ... },  // Full manifest.json content\n  \"download_urls\": {\n    \"initial_state\": \"https://s3.presigned.url/...\",\n    \"tick_chunks\": [\n      {\"range\": \"0-999\", \"url\": \"https://...\"},\n      {\"range\": \"1000-1999\", \"url\": \"https://...\"}\n    ],\n    \"checkpoints\": [...]\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### GET /replays/{game_id}/ticks\n**Stream tick data for a specific range**\n\n```\nGET /replays/{game_id}/ticks?start=0&end=1000\n\nResponse: (streaming JSONL)\n{\"tick\": 0, \"ts\": ..., \"cmds\": [...]}\n{\"tick\": 1, \"ts\": ..., \"cmds\": [...]}\n...\n```\n\n#### GET /replays/{game_id}/checkpoint/{tick}\n**Get nearest checkpoint for seeking**\n\n```\nGET /replays/{game_id}/checkpoint/1800\n\nResponse:\n{\n  \"checkpoint_tick\": 1800,\n  \"state_url\": \"https://s3.presigned.url/...\",\n  \"next_ticks_url\": \"https://s3.presigned.url/...\"\n}\n```\n\n### 4.3 Client-Side Reconstruction\n\nThe client performs local reconstruction using these steps:\n\n1. **Initialize**: Fetch manifest and initial state\n2. **Load Engine**: Initialize local game engine with `engine_version` and `physics_seed` from manifest\n3. **Apply State**: Load `initial_state.json.gz` into engine\n4. **Stream Ticks**: Fetch tick chunks and apply commands sequentially\n5. **Render**: Present each tick's resulting state to the player\n\n**Playback Algorithm (Pseudocode):**\n\n```python\nclass ReplayController:\n    def __init__(self, manifest, engine):\n        self.manifest = manifest\n        self.engine = engine\n        self.current_tick = 0\n        self.tick_buffer = []\n        self.playback_speed = 1.0\n\n    async def play(self):\n        # Load initial state\n        initial_state = await self.fetch_initial_state()\n        self.engine.load_state(initial_state)\n\n        # Start streaming ticks\n        tick_stream = self.stream_ticks(start=0)\n\n        while self.current_tick < self.manifest.total_ticks:\n            tick_data = await tick_stream.next()\n\n            # Apply all commands for this tick\n            for cmd in tick_data.cmds:\n                self.engine.apply_command(cmd)\n\n            # Advance engine simulation\n            self.engine.step()\n\n            # Render frame\n            self.renderer.present(self.engine.get_state())\n\n            # Control playback speed\n            await sleep(1.0 / (self.manifest.tick_rate_hz * self.playback_speed))\n\n            self.current_tick += 1\n\n    async def seek(self, target_tick):\n        # Find nearest checkpoint before target\n        checkpoint = self.find_nearest_checkpoint(target_tick)\n\n        if checkpoint:\n            state = await self.fetch_checkpoint(checkpoint.tick)\n            self.engine.load_state(state)\n            self.current_tick = checkpoint.tick\n        else:\n            # Replay from beginning\n            initial_state = await self.fetch_initial_state()\n            self.engine.load_state(initial_state)\n            self.current_tick = 0\n\n        # Fast-forward to target tick (no rendering)\n        while self.current_tick < target_tick:\n            tick_data = await self.get_tick(self.current_tick)\n            for cmd in tick_data.cmds:\n                self.engine.apply_command(cmd)\n            self.engine.step()\n            self.current_tick += 1\n```\n\n### 4.4 Integration with Existing Engine\n\nThe replay system reuses existing engine components:\n\n| Component | File | Usage in Replay |\n|-----------|------|-----------------|\n| Command processing | `ledgerquest/engine/core/command.py` | Apply recorded commands |\n| Physics simulation | `ledgerquest/engine/physics/simulator.py` | Deterministic tick advancement |\n| AI behavior | `ledgerquest/services/game_loop/ai_updater.py` | Re-execute AI decisions |\n| State serialization | `ledgerquest/engine/state/serializer.py` | Load/save checkpoints |\n| ECS system | `ledgerquest/engine/ecs/*.py` | Entity/component management |\n\nThe key insight is that the existing `PhysicsSimulator.step()` method (`simulator.py:267-288`) is explicitly designed to be **pure and deterministic**:\n\n```python\n# From ledgerquest/engine/physics/simulator.py:267-288\n@classmethod\ndef step(cls, bodies: List[RigidBody], delta_time: float, *, apply_gravity: bool = True) -> List[RigidBody]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Advance simulation by `delta_time` and return *mutated* bodies list.\n\n    The method is kept pure: no network, no I/O, no global state mutation,\n    which makes it deterministic and easy to unit-test.\n    \"\"\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. Architectural Impact and Risks\n\n### 5.1 Performance Impact on Live Game Loop\n\n#### Latency Analysis\n\n| Operation | Current Latency | Added Latency | Mitigation |\n|-----------|-----------------|---------------|------------|\n| `ProcessInput` | ~50ms | +5-15ms | Async S3 write |\n| `PersistState` | ~15-45ms | +0ms | No change |\n| Total tick | ~100ms | +5-15ms | Non-blocking pattern |\n\n**Mitigation Strategy: Non-Blocking Writes**\n\nInstead of synchronous S3 writes in the critical path, we propose:\n\n1. **Option A: Async Lambda Invocation**\n   - After `ProcessInput`, invoke a separate \"ReplayCapture\" Lambda asynchronously\n   - Zero added latency to game loop\n   - Eventual consistency (commands may be lost if Lambda fails)\n\n2. **Option B: Step Function Parallel State** (Recommended)\n   - Modify the state machine to add a parallel branch after `ProcessInput`\n   - One branch continues the game loop\n   - Other branch writes to S3\n   - Guaranteed delivery without blocking\n\n```json\n{\n  \"Type\": \"Parallel\",\n  \"Branches\": [\n    {\n      \"StartAt\": \"CaptureReplayData\",\n      \"States\": {\n        \"CaptureReplayData\": {\n          \"Type\": \"Task\",\n          \"Resource\": \"arn:aws:states:::lambda:invoke\",\n          \"Parameters\": {\n            \"FunctionName\": \"${ReplayCaptureFunction}\",\n            \"Payload.$\": \"$\"\n          },\n          \"End\": true\n        }\n      }\n    },\n    {\n      \"StartAt\": \"UpdateAI\",\n      \"States\": { /* existing game loop states */ }\n    }\n  ],\n  \"Next\": \"WaitForNextTick\"\n}\n```\n\n#### Memory Impact\n\n| Metric | Impact | Notes |\n|--------|--------|-------|\n| Lambda memory | +5-10 MB | Buffering 1 second of commands |\n| DynamoDB capacity | +1-2 WCU/game | Index writes only |\n\n### 5.2 Cost Analysis\n\n#### Storage Costs (Monthly, per 1000 active games)\n\n| Component | Calculation | Monthly Cost |\n|-----------|-------------|--------------|\n| S3 Standard (7 days) | 1000 games × 50MB × 7/30 | ~$0.03 |\n| S3 Standard-IA (83 days) | 1000 games × 50MB × 83/30 | ~$0.35 |\n| S3 Glacier (275 days) | 1000 games × 50MB × 275/30 | ~$0.18 |\n| DynamoDB Index | 1000 games × 1KB × 0.25 | ~$0.25 |\n| **Total Storage** | | **~$0.81/month** |\n\n#### Compute Costs (per 1000 game sessions)\n\n| Component | Calculation | Cost |\n|-----------|-------------|------|\n| Capture Lambda | 1000 games × 108K ticks × 128MB × 50ms | ~$0.90 |\n| Replay Lambda | Assuming 10% replay rate, 1000 requests | ~$0.02 |\n| API Gateway | 100 replay sessions × 1000 requests | ~$0.35 |\n| **Total Compute** | | **~$1.27/1000 sessions** |\n\n**Total incremental cost: ~$0.002 per game session**\n\n### 5.3 Risks and Mitigations\n\n#### Risk 1: Engine Determinism Violations (CRITICAL)\n\n**Risk**: If the game engine is not perfectly deterministic, replays will diverge from the original session.\n\n**Probability**: Medium — The codebase shows determinism intent but doesn't enforce it everywhere.\n\n**Impact**: High — Corrupted replays damage player trust.\n\n**Detection Indicators**:\n- `random.random()` calls without seeding (found in `ai_updater.py:127-129`)\n- Floating-point operations across different architectures\n- Hash iteration order in Python < 3.7\n- Time-dependent logic\n\n**Mitigations**:\n1. Introduce periodic checkpoints (every 60 seconds) to bound divergence\n2. Add determinism validation tests to CI/CD pipeline\n3. Log hash of game state every N ticks for divergence detection\n4. Version lock engine binaries in replay manifest\n\n#### Risk 2: Engine Version Compatibility (HIGH)\n\n**Risk**: Game engine updates may break playback of old replays.\n\n**Probability**: High — Game engines evolve continuously.\n\n**Impact**: High — Historical replays become unplayable.\n\n**Mitigations**:\n1. **Version tagging**: Store `engine_version` in manifest (`manifest.json`)\n2. **Engine versioning**: Package engine as Lambda Layers with version tags\n3. **Compatibility matrix**: Maintain documentation of breaking changes\n4. **Replay migration**: Build tooling to re-record old replays with new engine\n\n```python\n# Proposed engine version check in replay controller\nclass ReplayController:\n    def validate_compatibility(self, manifest):\n        current_version = self.engine.get_version()  # e.g., \"2.3.1\"\n        replay_version = manifest.engine_version      # e.g., \"2.2.0\"\n\n        if not is_compatible(current_version, replay_version):\n            raise IncompatibleReplayError(\n                f\"Replay requires engine {replay_version}, \"\n                f\"current engine is {current_version}\"\n            )\n```\n\n#### Risk 3: Storage Cost Explosion\n\n**Risk**: Long games or high-frequency events could generate excessive data.\n\n**Probability**: Low — Our estimates show ~50MB per hour.\n\n**Impact**: Medium — Cost overruns.\n\n**Mitigations**:\n1. Implement per-game-session size limits (e.g., 500MB)\n2. Add tenant-level quotas for total replay storage\n3. Aggressive lifecycle policies (Glacier after 30 days)\n4. Consider delta compression for command sequences\n\n#### Risk 4: Replay Data Loss\n\n**Risk**: Async writes may fail silently, resulting in incomplete replays.\n\n**Probability**: Low — S3 is highly durable.\n\n**Impact**: Medium — Incomplete replays.\n\n**Mitigations**:\n1. Use Step Function parallel branch (guaranteed execution)\n2. Implement end-of-game validation that checks tick count\n3. Mark replays as \"INCOMPLETE\" if validation fails\n4. DLQ for failed capture operations with retry logic\n\n### 5.4 Security Considerations\n\n| Concern | Mitigation |\n|---------|------------|\n| Multi-tenant isolation | S3 bucket policies enforce `tenant_id` prefix |\n| Data access | Pre-signed URLs with short TTL (15 minutes) |\n| Sensitive data in commands | Filter/redact chat messages before storage |\n| Replay manipulation | Include HMAC signature in manifest |\n| Rate limiting | API Gateway throttling (100 req/sec/tenant) |\n\n---\n\n## 6. Implementation Roadmap\n\n### Phase 1: Core Infrastructure (2-3 weeks)\n- [ ] Create S3 bucket with lifecycle policies\n- [ ] Create DynamoDB replay index table\n- [ ] Implement `ReplayCaptureService` Lambda\n- [ ] Modify Step Function to add parallel capture branch\n\n### Phase 2: Storage & Indexing (1-2 weeks)\n- [ ] Implement tick buffering and chunked writes\n- [ ] Implement manifest generation at game end\n- [ ] Add periodic checkpoint capture\n- [ ] Build replay index GSIs\n\n### Phase 3: API & Retrieval (2 weeks)\n- [ ] Implement Replay Service API endpoints\n- [ ] Add pre-signed URL generation\n- [ ] Implement streaming tick endpoint\n- [ ] Add CloudFront distribution (optional)\n\n### Phase 4: Client Integration (2-3 weeks)\n- [ ] Implement ReplayController in game client\n- [ ] Add seek functionality with checkpoint support\n- [ ] Implement playback speed controls\n- [ ] Build replay browser UI\n\n### Phase 5: Validation & Hardening (1-2 weeks)\n- [ ] Add determinism test suite\n- [ ] Implement divergence detection\n- [ ] Add monitoring and alerting\n- [ ] Load testing and optimization\n\n---\n\n## 7. Appendix\n\n### A. File References\n\n| File | Relevance |\n|------|-----------|\n| `infra/step_functions/game_loop_statemachine.asl.json` | Step Function definition to modify |\n| `ledgerquest/services/game_loop/input_processor.py` | Input capture integration point |\n| `ledgerquest/services/game_loop/state_committer.py` | Reference for S3 off-load pattern |\n| `ledgerquest/engine/state/serializer.py` | State serialization for checkpoints |\n| `ledgerquest/engine/physics/simulator.py` | Deterministic simulation reference |\n| `docs/architecture/adr/001-serverless-game-loop.md` | Determinism requirements |\n| `docs/architecture/data_model.md` | Existing event store patterns |\n\n### B. Alternative Approaches Considered\n\n| Approach | Pros | Cons | Decision |\n|----------|------|------|----------|\n| Full state capture every tick | Simple playback, no determinism requirement | 100x storage cost, high latency impact | Rejected |\n| State deltas only | Medium storage, simpler than input capture | Still larger than inputs, complex delta logic | Rejected |\n| DynamoDB Streams capture | Automatic, no code changes | High latency, expensive at scale, loses command context | Rejected |\n| EventBridge archive | Native AWS feature | Limited retention, no random access | Rejected |\n| **Input capture + checkpoints** | Low cost, leverages determinism, bounded divergence | Requires deterministic engine | **Selected** |\n\n### C. Open Questions\n\n1. Should replay data be encrypted at rest beyond S3 default encryption?\n2. What is the retention policy for e-sports/tournament replays vs casual games?\n3. Should we support partial replays (e.g., \"last 5 minutes\" only)?\n4. How do we handle replays for games with real-time voice chat?\n5. Should administrators have access to cross-tenant replay search?\n\n---\n\n*This document should be reviewed by the Platform Architecture team and Security team before implementation begins.*\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
